God of War Laufey is delayed until May 20

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Santa Monica Studio will use the extra time to push the game "to its fullest potential."

One of PlayStation's biggest first-party games of 2027 will be arriving later than expected as Santa Monica Studio has delayed God of War Laufey by three months. The PS5 exclusive was originally supposed to arrive on February 16 and it's now scheduled for May 20.

"Since the reveal of God of War Laufey in June, we've been deeply grateful to see so much excitement for Faye's journey through the Everywhen," the studio wrote in a social media post. "We don't take for granted the trust placed in our studio to make an extraordinary experience worthy of the passion and dedication of our fans. With this in mind, we've made the decision to delay God of War Laufey's release to May 20th, 2027. We will make the most of each moment until then by putting in the finishing touches and pushing the game to its fullest potential."

The delay will give God of War Laufey breathing room from a busy slate of releases at the beginning of next year, as well as Sony's own Until Dawn 2, which is out on January 28. The company typically releases each annual edition of MLB The Show in March as well. The delay ensures that Sony will have at least one first-party game out in the spring quarter of 2027.

In God of War Laufey, you'll play as Faye, whose death just before the opening of God of War (2018) kickstarts that game's story. Here, Faye reawakens in the afterlife and undertakes her own adventure that runs parallel with the exploits of her husband Kratos and son Atreus in God of War and God of War Ragnarök. There's also a talking jelly cube. I'm very excited for the talking jelly cube.

XRP Ledger Activates New Amendment to Strengthen Network

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The XRP Ledger (XRPL) network recently activated a new amendment that helps secure the network by resolving potential security weaknesses and making some existing functionalities more robust and reliable.

This latest amendment, called fixCleanup3_4_0, was activated on Nov. 4, bringing a series of technical updates that include fixes for token issuance, automated market makers (AMMs), lending functionalities, and other parts of the XRP Ledger network.

A fix for a potential security weak point related to token issuance on the XRP Ledger network is a critical aspect of the latest amendment.

As explained in the official documentation of the XRP Ledger network, this update addresses a potential weakness related to delegated permissions that could allow some accounts to issue tokens when they are only allowed to destroy them.

On the XRP Ledger, token issuers can grant permissions to other accounts to perform certain tasks. For example, a stablecoin issuer could delegate permission to burn tokens to another account when customers want to redeem them.

With the latest amendment, the XRP Ledger network addresses this security weakness by preventing a zero-balance, burn-only delegated payment from resulting in the issuance of new tokens.

On top of addressing token issuance permissions, there's more to the fixCleanup3_4_0 amendment.

As explained in the official XRP Ledger network documentation, the fixCleanup3_4_0 update brings several fixes to decentralized trading, asset management, and transaction processing.

For automated market makers (AMMs), this latest update prevents some invalid auction bids and makes some improvements to how liquidity withdrawals and token clawbacks work.

It also brings some fixes for potential unexpected calculation errors or rounding issues that could occur in some cases.

These changes are supposed to make things more predictable when it comes to liquidity pools and avoid potential failures that could happen in some cases.

Another significant aspect of these changes concerns Single Asset Vaults and the proposed native lending infrastructure on the XRP Ledger.

As explained, this amendment brings some improvements to how rounding is handled when assets enter or exit vaults.

The idea is to ensure that transactions do not result in more or fewer assets being transferred than they should.

Atmospheric AI Secures $10M Seed for Procurement Modeling Push

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Atmospheric AI announced on November 5, 2026, that it had raised $10 million in seed funding led by DBL Partners to expand its computational models of raw-material supply chains. DBL Partners published its portfolio page on November 4, 2026. The startup plans to move beyond plastics into chemicals, paper and metals, while DBL’s Vinnie Caruso joins its board.

The funding will also deepen collaboration with an initial group of around 100 manufacturers. That expansion connects the technical work to a commercial push: Dr Torsten Lichtenau is joining as founding chief commercial officer after 22 years at Bain & Company, where he was a senior partner and global head of carbon transition.

Atmospheric AI’s approach begins with the physical processes used to make materials, rather than depending on suppliers to disclose internal operating data. Its models incorporate chemical engineering, plant economics and industrial processes, including relationships among feedstocks, energy, production costs and emissions. The connected system covers oil fields and pipelines, refineries, chemical plants, terminals and shipping routes.

Atmospheric AI says it runs around 50,000 facility models and, in one test, reproduced a producer’s published carbon footprint to within 4.4% without using that producer’s own data. The models are intended to estimate what a material should cost, assess whether a supplier’s price increase is justified, identify other plants that could supply it and examine how a disrupted route could affect cost, carbon or resilience.

The company’s product description presents the platform as a dynamic digital twin of a raw-material value chain, from its origin to the factory gate. Facility-specific cost, carbon and risk information supports should-cost analysis for individual suppliers, supplier-specific Scope 3 emissions metrics and lifecycle-assessment integration. Multi-tier mapping, proactive alerts and sourcing recommendations are intended to bring disruption and negotiation decisions into the same workflow.

Haleon is one manufacturer already working with Atmospheric AI. Chris Low, Haleon’s head of sustainability procurement, said the companies have collaborated for almost two years on resin sourcing. His team now begins with a material’s should-cost, carbon footprint and supply-chain risks rather than relying only on the supplier’s quote, connecting the modeling work directly to a buying decision.

In a customer example described by Atmospheric AI, a manufacturer faced a proposed price increase of $730 per tonne during the Hormuz disruption. The startup’s team calculated that the underlying increase in manufacturing costs was approximately $120 per tonne. That analysis gave the sourcing team a basis for discussing what had changed in production economics instead of simply accepting the proposal or disputing it without evidence.

Co-founder and CEO Roee Goldberg described the long-term goal as a computational model of the raw-material economy: manufacturers would understand the plants and supply networks behind what they buy, with chemical engineering providing the foundation and AI enabling the work at a much larger scale.

The immediate test set by the round is breadth: whether a facility-based approach already applied to resin sourcing can support procurement decisions across additional material categories. Atmospheric AI is pairing that expansion with new commercial leadership and deeper manufacturer collaboration, making the move beyond plastics central to what the seed funding is meant to enable.

Progressives and moderates rejoice: 6 takeaways from the midterms

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Democrats were savoring their midterm election rout Wednesday after decisively flipping both the House and Senate.

“Last night was beyond even our largest expectations of what could happen,” Arizona Rep. Greg Stanton said on a press call.

Hakeem Jeffries, the top House Democrat in line to become the first Black speaker, predicted Democrats would hold at least 255 seats next year, which would be the largest majority for either party since Democrats won 257 seats in 2008.

In the Senate, the Democratic Caucus was poised to hold at least 54 seats after seismic red-state gains, with two key races too close to call.

“What we have now seen is a generational remake of the Senate map that is going to change America not only now but on into the future,” Charles E. Schumer, the chamber’s top Democrat, said Wednesday.

Here are six takeaways from the midterm elections:

Democratic gains were so sweeping that both ideological wings of the party had plenty to celebrate. The moderate Blue Dogs more than doubled their current membership, with at least 22 Blue Dog PAC-endorsed candidates winning Tuesday. Meanwhile, the Congressional Progressive Caucus anticipated at least 25 new members.

For House progressives, swing-seat wins in Michigan and California were signs that their candidates’ message resonated in battleground races and that Democrats should move to implement those progressive ideas next year.

“Big majorities can get big things done,” Congressional Progressive Caucus Chair Greg Casar said on a Wednesday press call.

Illinois Rep. Brad Schneider, the chair of the center-left New Democrat Coalition, told reporters he had a strong relationship with Casar and envisioned different factions of the party working together.

“It doesn’t matter what you call your idea. If your idea is going to help us move the ball forward, join us at the table,” Schneider said.

Army veteran Cait Conley, who was leading GOP Rep. Mike Lawler in a race that remained uncalled Wednesday afternoon, said she’d been open about having differences with incoming New York Democrats aligned with the Democratic Socialists of America. But she said Democrats across the political spectrum needed to prioritize “real solutions for the American people.”

“If we don’t make that our priority, if we don’t show that that is more important than anything else … then I think the voters are going to show us in 2028 that they’re not going to accept that,” she said on the New Dems press call.

While the president cast a shadow over the entire midterm map, three of his marquee candidates were among the night’s biggest busts.

Trump was behind appointed South Carolina Sen. Darline Graham every step of the way, helping her win a contested primary to replace her late brother, Lindsey Graham, on the ballot. But Graham lost to Democrat Annie Andrews in a stunning upset. In North Carolina, Trump had publicly urged Michael Whatley to run for the state’s open seat. But the former Republican National Committee chairman lost to Democratic former Gov. Roy Cooper by 20 points. And most significantly, in Texas, Trump elevated scandal-plagued state Attorney General Ken Paxton over Republican incumbent Sen. John Cornyn. But the president’s support wasn’t enough to stave off a general election drubbing at the hands of Democrat James Talarico.

Even Rep. Julia Letlow, Trump’s pick for Senate in deep-red Louisiana, won her election by less than 2 points.

Republicans in seven states successfully embarked on a mid-decade redistricting spree aimed at boosting GOP candidates. But the push couldn’t overcome the political headwinds that battered the Republican brand. In California, where Democrats launched a redistricting push in response to the Texas redraw, the new map proved more beneficial: Democrats are poised to flip at least four seats they redrew in their favor.

Republican Alex Mealer was the heavy favorite to win a redrawn Houston-area seat that Trump would have carried by 20 points. She raised $2.8 million to first-time Democratic candidate Leticia Gutierrez’ $215,000. Yet Gutierrez won the contest by double digits. In an interview on MS NOW, Gutierrez said her campaign proved that “you don’t need money to buy a congressional race.” She wasn’t the only under-the-radar House contender to pull off an unexpected victory Tuesday. In North Carolina, several low-profile and under-funded Democrats appear poised to pull off unlikely upsets, though the races remain too close to call.

Democrats have chased the dream of a blue Texas for more than 30 years — and always came up short. Until Tuesday. The party notched a series of stunning wins in the nation’s most consequential red state — from Talarico’s decisive victory in the Senate contest to winning several key House races to capturing the governor’s mansion. Democrats seized every elected statewide position on the ballot and flipped the state House, according to The Associated Press. “They said it could not be done,’’ Democratic Rep. Christian Menefee told supporters Tuesday night. “We did it.”

Democrats made deep gains in red states and districts across the country, flipping Senate seats in Texas, South Carolina and Kansas, while races in Alaska and Mississippi remained too close to call. Florida Gov. Ron DeSantis called it “probably the political worst case scenario for a midterm election.”

But Democrats didn’t appear convinced that their brand had completely rebounded from deep losses two years ago, saying they had work to do over the next two years.

“Many of the folks who voted for us yesterday, of course, are still skeptical,” Schumer said.

Similarly, Casar and Rep. Pramila Jayapal both warned Democrats not to assume that Tuesday’s wins had resolved the party’s branding issues.

“We still are not particularly popular as a Democratic Party across the country,” Jayapal told reporters. “I think we’ve got a shot to show that we can deliver on the kind of bold populist change that people ran on and won on.”

Meg’s Monster coming to PS5 on March 1, 2027

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Odencat will release a PlayStation 5 version of RPG adventure game Meg’s Monster on March 1, 2027, the companies announced.

Meg’s Monster first launched for Xbox One, Switch, and PC via Steam on March 2, 2023, followed by iOS and Android on May 7, 2026.

Additionally, the Meg’s Monster Complete Edition bundle containing the base game and downloadable content “Lost Memories” will be available for Switch on November 19. Both the downloadable content and bundle will be available for the PlayStation 5 version when it releases in 2027.

Here is an overview of the game, via Odencat:

Welcome to the Underworld—a land of monsters and mutants who eat humans for breakfast.

One such monster—a hulking ogre by the name of Roy—has no interest in eating humans. But one day, he and his best friend Golan stumble upon a lost little girl named Meg, and discover something truly terrifying: the moment she starts wailing in fear, a blood-red hue washes over the sky, and the earth itself begins to tremble and quake.

That’s right: this small girl’s tears hold the power to bring forth the apocalypse—and the only way to stop it is for them to help find her mother so she can make it safely back home.

Meg’s Monster is a short, story-driven JRPG with a big twist. Players control Roy, who begins the game with 99,999 HP and is virtually untouchable—but the real concern is keeping Meg safe, because if she starts crying, it’s game over for everyone. Players will have to master the unique mechanics and mini-games baked into each battle, all while protecting Meg and using her favorite toys to keep her pacified.

Over the course of their journey together, Roy and Meg will encounter a host of quirky and colorful characters, all lovingly rendered in Odencat’s trademark vibrant pixel aesthetic that makes even scary monsters look cute. There’s plenty of drama and intrigue to be found here—and even previously defeated enemies can be befriended through optional sub-events.

What begins as a relatively simple and heartwarming story about two unlikely friends ultimately expands into something much greater, as Roy and Meg slowly begin to unravel the mysteries surrounding the Underworld and themselves. The result is an emotional journey that, like any good storybook, will remain in your heart for years to come.

Park Hyatt Riviera Maya Cancun, Brand’s First All-Inclusive, Opens Soon

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Want to take advantage of Hyatt Privé benefits, including a room upgrade, complimentary breakfast, a hotel credit, and more, when booking a cash stay? Contact Ford ([email protected]) for more details. He may even be able to help if you already have a stay booked.

In late 2023, Hyatt and Parks Hospitality Holdings announced a collaboration that will expand Hyatt’s footprint in Mexico, with the development of four new properties. If you ask me, the most interesting of these is the new Park Hyatt Riviera Maya, which will also be the brand’s first all-inclusive property.

There’s now an update, as the hotel’s opening date has just been moved forward by a couple of months, and it’s now expected to open in the coming weeks.

The Park Hyatt Riviera Maya, located in Cancun, Mexico, is now accepting reservations for stays as of December 3, 2026. The hotel was initially supposed to open in 2025, then it started accepting reservations for stays as of February 2027, and now it’s accepting reservations for stays as of late 2026.

Hopefully the current opening date sticks, but if you book in the first several months the hotel is open, don’t be surprised if we see an additional delay, or at least don’t be surprised if not everything is ready.

The 148-key property will have a first-ever concept for the Park Hyatt brand, as it will be all-inclusive. Admittedly this is pretty normal for the Cancun area, but still, it’s noteworthy for a brand like Park Hyatt.

Hyatt has been leaning heavily into the all-inclusive space in recent times, particularly through acquisitions. So while Hyatt has grown massively in terms of its number of all-inclusive brands, it’s surprising to see even a traditionally non all-inclusive brand get this kind of an experience.

I’m generally not a fan of all-inclusives, or at least historically haven’t chosen to vacation at them. However, after my recent Ritz-Carlton Yacht Collection cruise, there is something I really enjoyed about not being nickel-and-dimed for everything, given how expensive luxury hospitality has become in so many places.

When it comes to the dining outlets, they’ll include Beach Grill IK’AL (the beachfront restaurant with seafood and Yucatecan flavors), Bruma (the steakhouse), Nutre Kitchen (the all-day dining restaurant), Pool Bar Brisa (the pool bar serving drinks & light snacks), Patisserie Market & Cafe (the coffee shop), Living Room (the lobby bar).

Accommodations will range in size from 559 square feet (52 square meters) to 2,303 square feet (214 square meters), so they’re generously sized, as you’d expect. The decor looks nice enough, but not like anything special.

As you’d expect, the resort will have amenities including several pools, a full-service spa, a gym, and more.

Once open, this adds to a growing number of luxury points hotels in the Cancun area. Just recently we saw the opening of the St. Regis Kanai and Waldorf Astoria Cancun, so it’s nice to see Hyatt’s most luxurious brand also expand to the Cancun area. As a matter of fact, the Park Hyatt will be immediately next to the Waldorf Astoria (which also recently became all-inclusive, as it wasn’t initially).

The Park Hyatt brand is really growing in Mexico right now. We’ve seen the recent opening of the Park Hyatt Los Cabos at Cabo Del Sol, while the Park Hyatt Mexico City is expected to open in early 2027. Beyond that, we also saw the recent opening of the Alila Mayakoba, another good luxury option in the Cancun area.

With the Park Hyatt Riviera Maya now bookable, what are rates like? If paying cash, rooms seem to generally start at around $1,500 per night, but that does include taxes & fees. So it’s obviously very expensive, but given Mexico’s high taxes & fees, it’s actually not quite as crazy as some may have expected.

Then again, I’m not necessarily expecting this to be typical Park Hyatt quality, given the extent to which this is outside the typical Park Hyatt wheelhouse. Oddly, at least as of now, rates seem to be the same whether one or two people are staying, which is unusual for an all-inclusive.

If you are looking to book a cash stay here, I’d recommend doing so through the Hyatt Privé program, which offers extra perks like room upgrades, a hotel credit, and more.

When redeeming points, the hotel follows World of Hyatt’s Category F all-inclusive award chart, which is the most expensive award pricing for all-inclusive properties.

Under Hyatt’s current award chart, the cost is typically 45,000 to 85,000 points per night, and that’s the same whether the room has single or double occupancy.

The Park Hyatt Riviera Maya is now accepting reservations for stays as of December 2026. The hotel is expected to be an all-inclusive, making it the first Park Hyatt property in the world where that’s the case.

It’s awesome to see Hyatt’s luxury expansion in Mexico, as this will be the third Park Hyatt to open in a fairly short time period. However, the Park Hyatt brand is becoming increasingly inconsistent, and I find the all-inclusive pivot to be a bit strange, especially given how many all-inclusive brands Hyatt already has.

What do you make of plans for the Park Hyatt Riviera Maya?

Zillow pushes Preferred agent program toward 100% of buyer connections

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Zillow reported Q3 2026 traffic down 3% to 215 million monthly users and a $158 million net loss. It highlighted AI mode expansion, Preferred growth and gains in mortgage and rentals revenue.  AI Summary

Zillow may be facing calls for investigations into some of its practices by consumer advocacy organizations and federal lawmakers, but the real estate listing portal giant is doubling down on its belief that it is “building the faster, easier and more affordable moving experience.”

“As more consumers and professionals use Zillow to move from dreaming to transacting, the old real estate experience will feel slow, difficult and expensive by comparison,”  Zillow CEO Jeremy Wacksman and Zillow COO and CFO Jeremy Hofmann wrote in a letter to shareholders published on Wednesday.

Despite this confidence, Zillow may be facing some headwinds. The company said that in the third quarter of 2026, visitor traffic to its apps and sites was down 3% during the quarter to 215 million average monthly unique users, while overall visits were down 5% annually to 2.3 billion according to Comscore data. Despite the declines, Comscore data shows that Zillow attracted 77% of average monthly unique visitors in the real estate category in Q3. In addition to this, the company said it has found that its AI search mode is deepening engagement, with visitors who use this option spending nearly three times as long as those who don’t on the site.

Zillow said that AI mode is now available to 100% of its signed-in users. The company said it was also exploring ways to use AI to make real estate professionals more productive and to help its internal engineers, who are using AI to ship 60% more changes to production than they did in 2024, according to Zillow.

“Buyers spend an average of two to three hours a week with us over about five months — and then for most, Zillow software and services power their transaction: the majority of residential real estate transactions in the U.S. run through our professional software,” the executives wrote. “Because of Zillow’s transaction infrastructure, our agentic AI does more than guide the shopping experience people love; it assists consumers and professionals throughout the transaction itself.

In addition to the decline in traffic, Zillow also reported a net loss of $158 million for the quarter, down from a $10 million net income generated in Q3 2025. This decline came even as revenue rose 7% annually to $724 million.

Zillow’s Preferred lead referral program is one of cornerstones of its revenue generation engine. The company reported that in Q3, two-thirds of buyer connections came to a Preferred agent partner in the integrated experience, roughly double what it was a year ago. Zillow said it was on track for Preferred to represent over three-quarters of connections by the end of the year, with it anticipating reaching 100% of connections in the first half of 2027. Zillow also noted that Preferred connections generate 35% more revenue per connection that its previous lead generation model.

Even with the growth of the Preferred program, Zillow’s for-sale revenue was flat year-over-year at $489 million. In addition to this, its residential segment recorded a 4% annual decline in revenue to $419 million, which the company attributed to higher-than-expected mortgage rates putting downward pressure on housing transaction volume.

Bright spots in Zillow’s Q3 earnings report occurred in its mortgage and rentals segments. Zillow’s mortgage rose 32% annually to $70 million, due to a 55% increase in purchase loan origination volume to $2.0 billion.

In markets where Zillow has integrated its mortgage product with its Preferred program the longest, Zillow said adoption rates of Zillow Home Loans is around 20%.

“We are also making Zillow Home Loans a trusted lender for Preferred agent partners’ entire book of business, not just for clients they connect with through Zillow,” Wacksman and Hofmann wrote.

Rentals revenue was up 26% year-over-year to $220 million, which the company attributed to multifamily revenue growing 35% year over year. Zillow said its rentals segment now accounts for roughly 30% of its total revenue, up from 21% two years ago. Additionally, the company noted that 81,000 multifamily properties were advertised on Zillow at the end of the third quarter, a 17% annual increase.

“Diversifying into this space has brought us a large growth opportunity and made our business more robust,” the executives wrote. “Our rentals strategy is to offer the broadest, most comprehensive inventory from single-family homes to large apartment buildings, and to streamline the transaction experience for consumers and housing providers.”

Executives added that they believe Zillow is still just scratching the surface of the opportunity presented by the rentals sector.

“We see a clear path to more than $1 billion in annual rentals revenue and a substantial opportunity to keep growing across both multifamily and longtail rentals well beyond that milestone,” Wacksman and Hofmann wrote.

The company also highlighted the performance of some of its other offerings, noting that its CRM product, Follow Up Boss, recorded roughly 141,000 monthly active users in Q3, up 16% annually. In addition to this, Zillow said over 800 brokerages had signed up for its pre-marketing product Zillow Preview and its listing enhancement product, Zillow Showcase, was on 5.4% of all new U.S. listings.

“Even as housing conditions deteriorated sharply, Zillow kept growing and outperforming, powered by our brand, audience, value to partners, disciplined cost management and strong growth levers across our well-diversified business,” Hofmann and Wacksman wrote. “These advantages are driving our performance through an exceptionally difficult macro environment today and position us to accelerate when conditions improve.”

Mexican Seafood and Natural Wines Are in the Spotlight at This New NYC Restaurant

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A stacked tuna tostada, puckering shrimp aguachile, and more seafood dishes stun at El Camarón Peludo

Known for their roasted and slow-cooked meats at Taqueria Ramirez, Tania Apolinar and Giovanni Cervantes partnered with chef Carlos Barrera to open a new seafood-focused wine bar this year. Here’s what a full day of prep looks like at El Camarón Peludo in the West Village.

First, fresh shrimp from Sinaloa, Mexico; Ecuador; and more locales are de-shelled and de-veined for three central shrimp dishes. Then, Cervantes starts the fresh salsa for the day, taste testing and comparing each batch to the salsa from last week to make sure the flavor is consistent. A bright red salsa is mixed with mayo to create the sauce on the camarones zarandeados, a shrimp version of a fish dish from the Pacific coast of Mexico that is usually grilled in a flat wire grill basket.

Upstairs, Apolinar prepares the wine bar for opening, laying out chilled seafood on ice in the window and wine bottles that will be placed in chilled buckets for service. She recommends sitting at the long, winding bar to watch the bartenders and chefs work.

Back in the kitchen, Barrera starts breaking down Spanish tuna for a creative tuna tostada layered with spicy mayo, fish eggs, habanero-lime oil, avocado, and crispy leeks. Then it’s taco prep, including the pescadilla (a deep-fried fish taco) and a shrimp taco with queso fresco, refried beans, and slices of roasted poblano peppers. Cervantes and Barrera prep the raw bar area, making salsa verde and salsa negro. The green salsa is made with parsley while the black salsa is layered with habaneros, piquin chile, and squid ink.

Cervantes explains that his original idea to pair the innovative seafood plates with bubbly wine turned into a natural wine bar, with El Camarón Peludo sourcing bottles from all over the world.

Europa League preview: AC Milan vs. Ferencvaros – Team news, opposition insight, stats and prediction

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

AC Milan are back in front of their own fans on Thursday night, hoping to correct their European course against Ferencvaros.

Milan are buoyed by the derby win over Inter at the weekend, and yet in continental competition they have struggled so far having lost two of three. So, the pressure remains on to bounce back and start climbing the Europa League table before it is too late.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

In his pre-match press conference, Ruben Amorim admitted that his side needed to find some consistency, especially after the win against Inter. Effectively, his message was that the win would mean little if it wasn’t followed with another three points.

Game date: Thursday, November 4 Kick-off time: 18:45 (CET) Venue: San Siro Referee: Urs Schnyder (VAR: Fähndrich)

As mentioned above, Milan could hardly be going into this game feeling any better after they scored three first-half goals to down their city rivals for a fourth straight time. Christian Pulisic, Adrien Rabiot and Diego Moreira scored in a 3-1 win that was well engineered by Ruben Amorim.

The Rossoneri continue to demonstrate their potential domestically, even if they remain seven points off top, but in the Europa League things haven’t gone to plan. Losses to Benfica and Bournemouth – both without scoring – either side of the rout of RB Salzburg leave them on three points from three games.

That puts Milan in 24th place in the table on goals scored, which is the final play-off spot. So, a big improvement is needed in the final five games of the league phase in order for the tag of being among the pre-tournament favourites to be justified.

Mario Gila, Rabiot and Moreira all returned to the line-up against Inter, which provided a very noticeable boost. However, there are no new recoveries in view of the match against Ferencvaros, so the depth is still limited.

Fikayo Tomori is in the treatment room after suffering a torn muscle in his right thigh, joining Matteo Gabbia, Alexis Saelemaekers and Samuel Chukwueze who also remain out. The latter may yet require surgery on a hip issue.

The other major absentee is Mike Maignan, who saw red for shoving Tyler Adams in the face during the last Europa League game against Bournemouth and will be forced to serve a one-match ban, which is less than feared.

The latest from the various media reports suggests that Amorim will field quite a youthful side, as he has done in Europe so far. This means chances should be given to Lorenzo Torriani, Sankhoun Diawara, Ardon Jashari, Davide Bartesaghi, Omari Hutchinson, Alphadjo Cisse and potentially more.

Expectations about rotation seem likely to be proven right, with the defence being almost completely changed. Without a back-up, Torriani is indeed given the nod.

Then, the backline is made of a largely rotated group. Filippo Terracciano will start at right-back, whilst Koni De Winter will partner Diawara in the middle of the back four. Bartesaghi returns at left-back.

In midfield, Hutchinson and Cisse will start on the flanks as expected, with Yunus Musah and Jashari given the nod in the middle of the park.

Despite suggestions that Camarda could start, it seems Goncalo Ramos will be given the starting role again.

Predicted Milan XI (4-4-1-1): Torriani; Terracciano, De Winter, Diawara, Bartesaghi; Hutchinson, Musah, Jashari, Cisse; Rabiot; Ramos

Predicted bench: Bouyer, Gila, Vladimirov, Modric, Pulisic, Camarda, Comotto, Pavlovic, Estupinan, Moreira,

Injuries & suspensions: Chukwueze, Gabbia, Tomori, Saelemaekers, P. Terracciano.

Ferencvaros went into last season as defending league champions under head coach Robbie Keane, but were forced to settle for second place. They did win the domestic cup though, and got to the last-16 of the Europa League before being beaten by Braga.

Keane left before the 2026-27 campaign, and he was replaced by Balazs Borbely who came from Gyor ETO, the club that have been one of the surprise packages in recent years, even winning the Nemzeti Bajnokság I (league title) last season.

It was quite a quiet summer for the Budapest-based team, as their big splash was Veljko Birmancevic from Sparta Praha for €2m. Matus Bero and Nathan Zohore arrived on free transfers, while Adam Nagy joined for a low fee from Spezia.

There were a few more exits though: Elton Acolatse, Zsombor Gruber, Mohammed Abu Fani, Gabi Kanichowsky and Cebrail Makreckis all left for figures between €2.5m and €1m, while five departed on free transfers too.

It has been a good start for Borbely, given that Ferencvaros are undefeated in their last 20 matches across all competitions, and they are dreaming of securing direct qualification to the last-16 of the competition.

The Zöldek have three wins from three, occupying fourth place in the overall standings behind Benfica, Bayer Leverkusen and Bournemouth. To qualify for the group stage, they overcame Twente, Gornik Zabrze and Trabzonspor, beating the latter 5-0 on aggregate.

An away win against Celtic kicked off their league phase, following by victories against Viktoria Plzen and Torreense. Overall, the Hungarian outfit have scored nine times and have conceded just three in what has been a perfect start in the UEL.

Ferencvaros’ only defeat came in their league debut on July 26 (2-4 against Baksi FC). Since then, they have gone on a long unbeaten streak of five wins and five draws, climbing to third place, just two points behind league leaders Gyor and with a game in hand.

In terms of injury news, the visitors will be missing Slovakian midfielder Matus Bero, while Bence Otvos is recovering from an ACL injury and will not take part. Aside from that, they have a clean bill of health.

Borbely’s side have so far alternated between a 4-1-4-1 formation – deploying former Liverpool player Nabi Keita or Adamy Nagy (who played for Bologna and Spezia) as midfielders – and a 4-2-3-1 to offer a bit more width.

A few rotations are expected from the weekend’s game – where they cruised to a 2-0 win – and the first set comes in defence. Radnoti, Nagy, Zohore and O’Dowda are all expected to return to the starting XI, with Gomez being the only survivor to retain his place.

Maiga will find a new partner at the base of the midfield in the aforementioned Nagy, whilst Zachariassen will start ahead of them.

On the wings, it’s an unchanged set-up with leading goalscorer Zachariassen and Birmancevic starting. Then, Yusuf is expected to return to the starting XI.

Predicted Ferencvaros XI (4-2-3-1): Radnoti; O. Nagy, Zohore, Gomez, O’Dowda; Maiga, A. Nagy; Arzani, Zachariassen, Birmancevic; Yusuf.

Predicted bench: A Varga, Dibusz, Osvath, Raemaekers, Cadu, Madrasz, Corbu, Bamidele, Bero, Joseph, Levi, Arzani, Rommens, Pappoe, Z Varga, Keita.

This will be the first ever competitive meeting between AC Milan and Ferencvaros, making it a historic encounter as a result.

Milan have played four times in history against Hungarian opponents. The first time was in the 1967-68 Cup Winners’ Cup, when the Rossoneri draw 2-2 away and 1-1 at home against ETO, which saw them progress on away goals.

The other two occasions came 15 years later in the 1982 Mitropa Cup group stage against Szombathelyi Haladas. The Diavolo won both encounters: 2-0 at San Siro, and 1-0 away from home.

➤ Alphadjo Cisse has caught the eye for Milan in Europa League play. The striker is the side’s top scorer with three goals, which is joint most in the competition, one of those having been the critical first of the match. Kristoffer Zachariassen has been the most prolific player for Ferencáros, scoring on three occasions in the current Europa League campaign, including one match-opening goal. He is tied as the competition’s top scorer in 2026/2027.

➤ AC Milan will look to build on their derby success when they welcome an unbeaten Ferencváros side to the Stadio Giuseppe Meazza in the UEFA Europa League on Thursday evening. Ruben Amorim’s team have enjoyed a promising domestic campaign but struggled to replicate that form in Europe, occupying 24th place in the league-phase standings with just three points from their opening three matches. Their Hungarian opponents, meanwhile, sit fourth with a perfect nine-point return and arrive in Italy chasing a fourth consecutive European victory.

➤ The Rossoneri secured an impressive 3-1 victory over city rivals Inter Milan at the weekend, strengthening their position in the Serie A top-four race. Milan have lost just once in their opening ten league matches, recording six victories and three draws, although their continental performances have been considerably less convincing. They suffered a comprehensive 3-0 defeat away to Bournemouth on matchday three, leaving them with two losses from their opening three Europa League fixtures and only one victory in their last six European matches overall.

➤ Milan’s struggles have extended to home soil, where they lost 2-0 against Benfica in their only previous Europa League fixture at the Giuseppe Meazza this season. Another defeat would see them lose consecutive European home matches within a single campaign for the first time since 2010/11. Nevertheless, their recent attacking form offers some encouragement: five of their last six competitive matches have produced at least three goals, and Amorim’s side has scored before the 25th minute in each of their previous four home fixtures.

➤ Ferencváros, by contrast, have enjoyed an outstanding start to their European campaign, winning all three league-phase matches and scoring freely along the way. Their most recent outing produced a convincing 4-1 victory over Torreense, with Bamidele Yusuf, Mariano Gómez and Krisztián Lisztes among the scorers. Balázs Borbély’s side have now won five of their last six competitive matches, drawing the other, and have scored 18 goals while conceding just four during that sequence.

➤ The Hungarian champions are unbeaten in 20 competitive matches since late July, recording 13 victories and seven draws, while each of their last four wins has come by at least two goals. They also boast an impressive away record, having avoided defeat in their last ten competitive matches on the road and conceded no more than once in any of those fixtures. Victory in Milan would see Ferencváros win four consecutive matches in major European competition for the first time since the 1967/68 Inter-Cities Fairs Cup, when they recorded six successive victories on their way to the final.

➤ This will be the first competitive meeting between Milan and Ferencváros, although both clubs have limited historical experience against opposition from the other’s country. Milan’s only previous European encounters with Hungarian opponents came against Győri ETO in the 1967/68 Cup Winners’ Cup, with both matches ending level. Ferencváros, meanwhile, have failed to win any of their last four European away fixtures against Italian teams, drawing twice and losing twice, since defeating Roma in the 1965 Inter-Cities Fairs Cup. With Milan seeking to revive their European campaign and the visitors determined to preserve their perfect record, both sides have considerable motivation heading into this encounter.

Sankhoun Diawara has been superb so far when called upon, and he’ll replace the ever-present Strahinja Pavlovic here. A big performance would not only reassure the club of his ability (and potential), but it may offer a reason to change January plans.

It feels like a weekly occurrence now, but Goncalo Ramos needs a goal. If Francesco Camarda is not going to start here, the Portuguese needs to prove that there is reason to keep the youngster on the bench. For his sake, as much as the Italian’s.

For the visitors, Kristoffer Zachariassen is the main danger man. Contributing to 11 goals in 21 games so far this season, the 32-year-old will be essential to Ferencvaros getting anything on Thursday.

Then, Mariano Gomez will represent a difficult opponent for whoever leads the Rossoneri’s line. The Argentine defender has been excellent so far this season, and it’s entirely possible that he contributes to a frustrating night.

Despite their rotations, Milan must win here; if they don’t, they’ll wave their Europa League top eight wishes goodbye. There’s enough quality in the starting XI to provide three points, so that will be the expectation.

« Une immense tristesse » : Le FC Metz et le football français pleurent Carlo Molinari

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Le FC Metz et le football français sont en deuil ce mercredi 4 novembre 2026. Le club mosellan a officialisé la disparition de son ancien président emblématique Carlo Molinari, décédé à l'âge de 93 ans. Une figure majeure de l'histoire grenat s'en va.

L'annonce de la disparition de Carlo Molinari a suscité une profonde émotion en Lorraine ainsi qu'au sein du football français. Sur ses réseaux sociaux, le FC Metz a immédiatement tenu à saluer la mémoire de son ancien dirigeant, qui a façonné l'identité du club au fil des décennies. Un choc immense pour la maison grenat.

L'ensemble des équipes du club s'est rassemblé pour rendre hommage à l'ancien président. « Le président Bernard Serin, les dirigeants, les joueurs, les entraîneurs, les anciens du club et l'ensemble des salariés du FC Metz adressent leurs plus sincères condoléances à sa famille et à ses proches. Merci Président Molinari, pour votre engagement, votre fidélité, votre humanité et tout ce que vous avez apporté au FC Metz. Votre histoire restera à jamais liée à celle des Grenats », a ainsi transmis le FC Metz dans son hommage officiel.

Carlo Molinari a dirigé le club mosellan pendant trente-quatre ans au total, au cours de deux mandats distincts : de 1967 à 1978, puis de 1983 à 2009. Fils d'immigré italien, cet ancien champion de France de cyclo-cross avait fait fortune dans une concession de poids lourds avant de succéder à Paul Mayer à la présidence du club lorrain en 1967. Sous sa gouvernance, la formation mosellane a garni son palmarès en remportant deux Coupes de France, en 1984 et en 1988, ainsi que deux Coupes de la Ligue, en 1986 et en 1996. Une longévité remarquable au plus haut niveau.

Au-delà de ces titres nationaux, le parcours du dirigeant reste gravé dans les mémoires grâce à l'exploit européen du 3 octobre 1984 en Coupe des vainqueurs de coupe. Battus à domicile au match aller (2-4), les Messins avaient renversé le FC Barcelone au Camp Nou en s'imposant largement (1-4) pour décrocher une qualification historique. D'ailleurs, les Lorrains ont aussi terminé à la deuxième place du championnat de Division 1 en 1998, échouant seulement à la différence de buts derrière le RC Lens.

Après avoir cédé les commandes du FC Metz à Bernard Serin avant de prendre du recul, Carlo Molinari avait d'abord occupé le rôle de vice-président tout en conservant une présence constante auprès de son club de cœur. Figure paternelle, il a notamment participé à l'éclosion d'internationaux français majeurs tels que Patrick Battiston et Robert Pirès. L'ancien dirigeant avait d'ailleurs assisté fin septembre 2026 au jubilé de Robert Pirès au stade Saint-Symphorien. Une fidélité absolue envers ses protégés.

En effet, le dirigeant entretenait une proximité très forte avec toutes les strates de l'institution. « Il connaissait l'importance de celles et ceux qui, dans l'ombre comme dans la lumière, contribuent chaque jour à faire vivre un club de football. Passionné, entier, parfois exigeant, mais toujours profondément attaché au FC Metz, il entretenait avec cette institution une relation qui dépassait largement le cadre de ses fonctions. Le club était pour lui une véritable famille », a détaillé le FC Metz au sujet de son ancien président. Actuellement dixième de Ligue 2, l'équipe messine disputera son prochain match le vendredi 6 novembre 2026 à 20h00 face au Montpellier HSC au Stade de la Mosson, dans le cadre de la 12e journée, et rendra certainement hommage à son légendaire président.

God of War Laufey delayed three months to May 2027, as Sony Santa Monica escapes a packed February

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

"We will make the most of each moment until then."

God of War Laufey has been delayed. Sony Santa Monica's PS5 exclusive is now dodging the crowded month of February and will instead come out on 20th May, 2027.

Developer Sony Santa Monica announced it's pushing back the upcoming action epic via a brief social media post. While the studio stopped short of giving a reason for the delay, it did say it's using the extra time to polish Faye's solo adventure.

"Since the reveal of God of War Laufey in June, we've been deeply grateful to see so much excitement for Faye's journey through the Everywhen. We don't take for granted the trust placed in the studio to make an extraordinary experience worthy of the passion and dedication of our fans," the studio wrote.

"With this in mind, we've made the decision to delay God of War Laufey's release to May 20th, 2027," the statement continued. "We will make the most of each moment until then by putting in the finishing touches and pushing the game to its fullest potential. On behalf of everyone on the team, we appreciate your patience and we'll be back to share more updates soon."

God of War Laufey is notably the first game in the famed PlayStation series to not star Kratos in the lead role. This time around, Faye takes the lead through a version of the afterlife that houses gods and deities from multiple mythologies - and it's bringing back the original series' faster combat, too.

Of course, Kratos will return in future God of War games (he also shows up here and there during Faye's romp), and whatever that next Kratos game becomes, it'll supposedly connect to the events of Laufey, according to the developer.

On the bright side, the delay might be good news for anyone worried about their impossibly large and ever expanding backlogs. February 2027 is already packed with really exciting games - from the Fable reboot and Persona 4 Revival to Metro 2039. Hopefully, Laufey's delay gives everyone more breathing room.

Oblivion and Skyrim’s gap was bridged by forward-looking books about a murderous floating island, so why is The Elder Scrolls 6 stuck reanimating a world that’s stood still since 2011

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Why the Umbri-hell has time stopped in Tamriel?

Long waits between mainline entries in a series aren’t a new phenomenon, if perhaps a bit more common nowadays due to big games' ballooning development cycles. It took about 23 years for BioWare’s Baldur's Gate 2 to be followed up by Larian’s Baldur’s Gate 3, though there were a decent amount of spin-offs and remasters peppered between the two.

The wait for The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel hasn’t stretched on that long quite yet, but as Bethesda finally revealed concrete details about it just in time for Skyrim's 15th birthday, it really hit home for me that TES VI will be tasked with restoring motion to a world where calendars have been stuck in the 201st year of the 4th era since 2011. Perhaps it’s because I’m still in my twenties and haven’t lived long enough to see that many things I like pause their forward march for this long before returning. There’s also a good chance my expectations of timelines rolling forward on a regular basis without the need for a whole new entry are coloured by the constant stream of new stuff fed into most games via updates and live service passes these days.

However, when I look back at the interregnum between the last two Elder Scrolls games, Oblivion and Skyrim, I can’t help but feel that what Bethesda and ZeniMax have packed into the gap between Skyrim and Sentinel might have done the latter no favours. Sure, The Elder Scrolls Online has arguably meant that there’s been far more for series fans to sink their teeth into between mainline games than ever before, but because the MMO's a prequel, none of its tales and lore push the Elder Scrolls' timeline forwards. Meanwhile mobile and card game spin-offs like The Elder Scrolls: Blades, The Elder Scrolls: Legends, and The Elder Scrolls: Castles have all either slotted in before Skyrim or not been tied to a specific point in time.

On one hand, that leaves the devs a nice clean slate to take the sixth entry wherever they like narratively upon the conclusion of the Dragonborn’s adventures. On the other, Bethesda has missed the opportunity to paint out the world and further set up the stakes for Sentinel in the interim. Back when we were waiting for Skyrim's original release, the developers were pumping out spin-off books that daubed some fresh adventures onto the Elder Scrolls' canvas.

I bought these books - 2009’s The Infernal City and 2011’s Lord of Souls - ages ago upon hearing their tales referenced in the sorts of Elder Scrolls lore videos which appeal to sad nerds of my ilk, but have only gotten around to reading past the opening chapters in recent months.

In hindsight, I think I bounced off them that first time because it just felt a bit weird to be reading about set characters getting up to adventures in Tamriel rather than setting off on them in the boots of my own custom created protagonist. I didn’t run into the same with The Witcher, as you’re seeing that world through Gerry from the River’s eyes no matter where you turn. Anyway, the two Elder Scrolls novels by American author Greg Keyes tell the tale of the Umbriel crisis which took place 48 years after the Oblivion crisis and about 153 years before Skyrim’s dragon crisis (Tamriel does love a crisis). The Umbriel crisis saw a floating island powered by souls and formed via some Daedric drama, carve a path of destruction from Black Marsh to the Imperial City. Yep, the empire’s not-Roman soldiers had to fight a flying hunk of land. At one point some mages had to float a legion up in the sky so they could get their bottoms kicked by Umbriel’s defenders.

As you might’ve gathered, the books take the opportunity to tell a story which - especially in terms of where tech was in 2009 - wouldn’t necessarily fit an Elder Scrolls game. Or, at least, not one of the more straight-laced variety Bethesda have made in their post-Morrowind days. A lot of time’s spent looking through the eyes of two characters who’ve snuck aboard Umbriel, the floating island, after it destroyed their hometown and have to adjust to the strange customs of its inhabitant lords and their underlings before finding ways to help take it down. On the ground, things take a more familiar form, as a very serious Dark Elf named Sul and a nepo baby Imperial prince trek across bits of Cyrodiil, Morrowind, and Elsweyr on a quest to do their bit in vanquishing the threat. Then, there’s a Penitus Oculatus spy named Colin trying to figure out if the Umbriel Crisis is an inside job of some sort and a few tales of regular Imperial troops working to evacuate citizens living in the floating island’s path. In an Elder Scrolls game, you’d get some of that, but not be able to hop from person to person chapter by chapter in the fashion the books do.

Umbriel slowly floats across Black Marsh, southern Morrowind and Cyrodiil with an army of zombies in tow, much like this article’s about to float into mildly spoilery territory for the two books. You see, The Infernal City and Lord of Souls outline what happened to cause the red year, a volcanic eruption which took the land of the Dark Elves from the beautifully alien place it was at the time of The Elder Scrolls 3 and reduced it to the ash-covered wasteland it’s outlined to be at the time of Skyrim. As the latter game’s lore books would subsequently set in stone, Baar Dau - the meteor suspended in the air above Vivec City at the time of Morrowind - fell and destroyed the city following the disappearance of the metropolis’ namesake ex-Tribunal god-king. The books reveal that the big rock didn’t fall the moment Vivec vanished, but was kept afloat by a device called the Ingenium, which is essentially a magic box powered by regular soul sacrifices. Two Dunmer, Vuhon and the aforementioned Sul, ended up having a fight rooted in the tech’s murderous nature and that’s actually what led Baar Dau to finally hit the ground.

Either way, the meteor falls, but that's not an insubstantial bit of backstory for a source outside of the games to add to the Elder Scrolls’ canon. Also wound into the tale of how Umbriel came to be is a bunch of extra flesh on the bone of the relationship between Daedric prince Clavicus Vile and Umbra. In the games, the latter’s always just been a powerful sword which imprints a new personality onto its wielder, but in the books it’s portrayed as a being capable of nicking some of Vile’s godly power and plotting to escape the prince’s realm of Oblivion via the building of Umbriel. Beyond those, the books also offer a chance to get to know emperor Titus Mede I, the warlord who took over the Empire following a post-Oblivion crisis interregnum and whose offspring you murder during Skyrim’s Dark Brotherhood questline.

None of this is anything close to required knowledge for playing Skyrim. No characters from the books pop up to say hello, likely because enough time’s passed for most to perish of natural causes. The sole reference I can recall comes via a chat about a location called Sleeping Tree Camp, which isn’t tied to any of the main questlines. You can, and I did for a long time, totally ignore these time-bridging tomes. That said, I do feel that having now taken the time to read them has helped me see the history of Tamriel as something not as strictly limited to the specific snapshots in time presented by each of the games.

Tales like these, which aren’t conveyed with as much natural emotional detachment as I tend to feel when reading much-condensed accounts of historical events or far off places on the pages of in-game lore books, really help in creating the illusion that The Elder Scrolls’ world is growing and evolving as our own does. It’s not just a snow globe that only shifts whenever some prophesied hero piloted by a controller or keyboard rocks up to shake it.

I do think, in large part due to the size of the real world gap between it and Skyrim, The Elder Scrolls 6 would have and still could benefit from having spin-offs like these to keep gently nudging the timeline forwards. A complicating factor is that judging by this week’s teaser, Sentinel seems like it could be set in the 206th year of the fourth era, a mere five years after the events of Skyrim. That’d mean no 200-ish years of wiggle room in which to slot some spin-off stories without serious risk of influencing things that’d have to be reflected in the new game. Unless Bethesda wanted to either put serious barriers up around where the spin-off could tread or get into a game of arbitrarily deciding what is or isn’t canon. With some collaboration, it could certainly be done, but it could also be a headache the studio wouldn’t want.

It’s likely too late to start the ball rolling now anyway, but that doesn’t stop it being worth asking why Bethesda and their famously adaptation-loving corporate bosses at Microsoft didn’t make sure they had something akin to these books in the works when Sentinel was punted down the road in favour of Starfield.

You did this well once, why haven’t you managed to pull it off again this time? I’m hoping that isn’t the inquiry which’ll come to define The Elder Scrolls 6 as much as questions about where a chunk of the old Bethesda magic had gone did with the studio’s trip to space. Lest the next long wait for a game from them be packed with even more uncomfortable silence.

“War” Secretary Pete Hegseth Endorses 15 Minute City Model

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

You just can’t make this stuff up. While conservatives around the world are waging holy war against the “15 Minute City” concept, US Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth has gone all-in on the idea. His newly announced “Fortress America” military resilience plan includes a new vision for military bases that embraces walkability and quality of life. If that sounds like the 15 Minute City plan in action, it is. Oh, lordy…

Walkable communities are as old as the hills. That’s what people did before automobiles hit the scene and cities were organized around cars. Credit for applying the walkability model to 21st century urban planning goes to the Franco-Colombian scientist Carlos Moreno, who formalized the 15 Minute City concept in 2016. The idea got a further boost of attention when the Mayor of Paris, Anne Hidalgo, pitched walkable, bike-able neighborhoods as part of her successful re-election campaign in 2020.

The COVID-19 lockdown of 2020 was also a pivotal moment, with a surge in work-from-home households drawing attention to the need for local resources as a matter of survival and resiliency in case of a widespread emergency.

The 15 Minute City model covers a lot of ground, but the basic idea is that people should be able to do their shopping, recreation, socializing, and other essential routines within walking or biking distance from their homes, making cars largely optional within the neighborhood. Walkable access to schools and health care is also part of the plan.

The argument for 15-minute cities (also called 15-minute neighborhoods or 15-minute communities) is also pretty straightforward. Walking is healthier than sitting in a car. Avoiding injury or death from car accidents is another plus. Walking also reduces carbon emissions, and it saves money on gas.

In addition, walkability levels the playing field, so to speak. Everyone in the 15-minute zone has walkable access to basic resources, regardless of whether or not they can afford a car. In other words, there is a significant equity and inclusion element to the concept…

…and, that’s where the trouble kicks in. In 2022 the World Economic Forum posted an article by Lisa Chamberlain, the founder of the urban communications and business firm Strategies for City Builders. Chamberlain describes a 15 Minute City as characterized by diversified, mixed-use neighborhoods built to withstand shocks like COVID-19 and climate change.

“As climate change and global conflict cause shocks and stresses at faster intervals and increasing severity, the 15-minute city will become even more critical,” Chamberlain emphasized, with a further emphasis on equity and inclusion:

“Specifically, communities that foster and maintain social and economic relationships don’t have to be wealthy, but they do need to be walkable and safe, with both residential and commercial buildings intact. And, I would add, for 15-Minute Cities to thrive, not just survive crises—and this cannot be stressed enough—they must also have plenty of mixed-income and equitable housing, as well as digital access.”

It didn’t take long for the conspiracy machine to kick into action around the globe. In February of 2023, for example, the Australian news organization ABC radio summed up the 15 Minute City conspiracy theory thusly:

“To some, it’s an urban planning concept that promotes sustainable and healthy living.

“To a small group of others, it’s a plot by ‘tyrannical bureaucrats’ to take our cars and control our lives, which could lead to a real-life Hunger Games scenario.”

“Limiting cars and promoting pedestrian or bike access is framed as a slippery slope to government-run, open-air prisons,” ABC continued.

Yikes! ABC also cites the Canadian psychologist Jordan Peterson. who railed against “idiot tyrannical bureaucrats” who “decide by fiat where you’re ‘allowed’ to drive…and, make no mistake, it’s part of a well-documented plan” (emphasis added).

Ohfergawdsakes, of course there is a well documented plan, and it’s called urban planning. And, that’s where the US Department of Defense comes in.

On September, in a move that sailed under the CleanTechnica radar, Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth announced the new “FORTRESS America” plan, with FORTRESS representing “Federal Operations for Resilience, Troop Resources, Energy, Supply, and Survivability.”

Stripping away Secretary Hegseth’s testosterone-fueled purple prose in the September 30 announcement, FORTRESS AMERICA applies the 15 Minute City urban planning model to major Defense Department bases in the US, ensuring they can operate independently of civilian infrastructure for a sustained period of time. That includes everything needed to support community life in addition to the supplies needed to sustain military operations.

FORTRESS America even goes beyond the initial 15 Minute City concept. As envisioned under the “Next Great American Base” element of the program, a central feature of the plan consists of access to a reliable source of on-site energy.

Don’t just take my word for it. “The program aligns basing and infrastructure projects to ensure they are collectively advancing toward greater independence,” Hegseth stated in the official order, with independence referring to the capability to sustain operations within a facility that is cut off from civilian resources. In other words, sustainable urban planning for military facilities.

Hegseth tasked the Under Secretary of War for Acquisition and Sustainment to carry out the new order. The base design part of the order was passed along to Dale Marks, the Assistant Secretary for Energy, Installations, and Environment.

Marks did not let the 15 Minute City grass grow under his feet. In a public presentation on October 29, he emphasized the importance of building quality of life into a sustainable model for military facilities in the US.

“Let us build installations worthy of the warriors who call them home,” Marks emphasized. “The nation shouldn’t expect warfighters to focus on high stakes, multidomain operations when they come home to base housing with black mold, failing plumbing and leaky roofs.”

“Substandard living conditions are not minor inconveniences. They are an active drain on morale, combat readiness and our retention of top-tier talent,” Marks elaborated.

It’s not just a matter of rehabbing military housing. In the presentation, Marks made the case for a full blown 15 Minute City model. “The Next Great American Base will include a compact, walkable concept with premier schools, advanced healthcare and town centers that foster community,” Marks continued, neatly summing up the fundamentals of 15 Minute Cities in a nutshell.

Marks anticipates that state governors and other stakeholders will compete to see where the first “Next Great American Base” will be constructed. Once complete, the new facility, and its 15 Minute City, will serve as a model for future projects.

As for the on-site power generation angle, nuclear energy gets the pole position — for now. As Marks and Hegseth both emphasize, though, speed is of the essence. Results are demanded within months, if not weeks. That leaves plenty of room for solar-enabled microgrids to throw their hat into the FORTRESS America ring. After all, if speed-to-power is the name, solar and batteries are the game…

Image: The 15 Minute City model calls for walkable, bike-able access to basic services and resources, a concept that has been embraced by none other than the US Department of Defense (courtesy of Rutgers University).

Here Are the Cable News Ratings for October 2026

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

MS NOW and CNN reversed fortunes as both networks finished October on an upward swing in total viewers and the Adults 25-54 demo during primetime and total day. This uplift comes after two back-to-back months of viewership losses.

MS NOW also made recent history, topping CNN in the demo during weekend live programming (7 a.m.-6 p.m. ET) for the first time in 14 years, since November 2012. MS NOW had 50,000 vs. CNN’s 48,000 demo viewers.

The network also surpassed CNN in the total day demo, moving ahead of the Skydance-owned channel courtesy of 2,000 additional demo viewers.

Meanwhile, Fox News had its third consecutive month of viewership losses in both measured categories during both dayparts.

According to Nielsen Big Data + Panel, Fox News had 1.675 million total viewers in primetime and 145,000 viewers in the adults 25-54 demo in October. During total day, it had 1.101 million total viewers and 98,000 viewers in the demo.

Compared to its September 2026 performance, Fox News was down 2% in total viewers and 7% in the A25-54 demo during primetime. In total day, the network fell 1% in total viewers and 8% in the demo compared with the previous month.

Looking at Fox News’ performance against the same month in 2025, it declined by 28% in total viewers and 36% in the demo during primetime. While in total day, it was down by 27% in total viewers and 32% in the demo—the only cable network with year-over-year declines in both measured categories.

The network finished October in second place among total viewers and was fourth in the demo during primetime. During total day, it remained in first place in total viewers and was No. 3 in the demo.

MS NOW finished second among news networks in total viewers in primetime and total day, with 925,000 and 588,000 viewers, respectively, in October. In the A25-54 demo, the network had 95,000 viewers in primetime and 62,000 during total day.

Compared to September, MS NOW was up 10% in total viewers and 20% in the A25-54 demo during primetime. During total day, it also had increases of 8% in total viewers and 19% in the demo.

Looking at its performance in October 2025, MS NOW was up 13% in total viewers and 30% in the demo during primetime. It was also up 17% in total viewers and 38% in the demo during total day.

MS NOW was the No. 5 cable network during primetime and No. 3 during total day among total viewers. It finished 14th in the demo during primetime and jumped from 11th to eighth during total day.

In October primetime, CNN had 598,000 total viewers and 107,000 A25-54 viewers. Looking at total day, the network had 402,000 total viewers and 60,000 demo viewers.

Compared to the previous month, CNN grew by 11% in total viewers and 19% in demo viewers during primetime. It was up by 8% in total viewers and 13% in the demo during total day.

When looking at its performance to the same month in 2025 during primetime, CNN had gains of 19% in total viewers and 39% in the demo. It was also up 4% during total day and 3% in the demo.

CNN finished sixth in primetime and fifth in total day in total viewers among all cable networks. In the demo, it finished up two spots to ninth in primetime and was also ninth in total day.

Canon Technology Used to Perform World’s First Coral ‘Pregnancy Test’

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Canon Technology Used to Perform World’s First Coral ‘Pregnancy Test’

Coral Spawning International (CSI), in partnership with Canon Medical Systems UK and Canon EMEA, has successfully conducted the world’s first ultrasound scan of living coral to identify developing eggs.

At a time when coral conservation is of paramount importance, this is the first non-invasive ultrasound scan on living, submerged corals that has been completed. It was conducted with the Aplio i700, an advanced Canon ultrasound platform. Scientists explain that accurately detecting egg development in living colonies allows researchers to better predict spawning windows, accelerating lab-based propagation, selective breeding, and reef restoration efforts.

In ultrasound technology, ultra-high frequency provides a sharper, high-resolution picture. However, this sharpness sacrifices the depth the sound waves are able to travel. As such, a mixture of both high (deeper but lower resolution) and ultra-high (shallow but high resolution) frequencies is used to gain a complete picture of the corals’ internal structures.

“Corals still hold so many secrets, particularly when it comes to the complex processes that drive their reproduction. Being able to look inside a living coral and see these processes unfolding in such remarkable detail is an extraordinary moment. This experiment has given us a new perspective on these animals and, as we continue monitoring them through spawning, we hope to build an increasingly detailed picture of how different coral species reproduce,” Dr. Jamie Craggs, Co-Founder of Coral Spawning International, says.

“This experiment showcased just how powerful ultrasound technology can be. Moreover, that the experiment was successful opens opportunities in fields such as minimally invasive surgery and diagnosis of dermal conditions,” Sally Davey, Clinical Product Specialist, Canon Medical Systems UK, adds.

Using Canon’s Aplio i700 hospital ultrasound system with a mix of high and ultra-high frequency probes, researchers successfully imaged internal egg structures in six large polyp stony (LPS) coral species underwater, which eliminates the traditional need to cut open or sacrifice living corals to check their reproductive state. The scan findings were verified on Trachyphyllia geoffroyi coral by cross-referencing the ultrasound images with macro photography.

While this achievement is impressive on its own, Canon says that the success of high-resolution underwater ultrasound provides valuable insights for human medical fields, including minimally invasive surgery and dermal diagnostics.

“This experiment demonstrates that imaging innovation has the power to show us what was previously unseen, creating deeper understanding of the world around us. By unlocking this knowledge, we hold a fundamental belief that imaging has the power to transform the world,” Peter Bragg, Sustainability and Government Affairs Director at Canon EMEA, explains.

Canon adds that the corals used in this experiment will continue to be monitored for six months, with recordings taken when gametes (mature reproductive cells) are released. This will allow CSI to better understand the coral reproductive cycle, helping identify spawning windows more accurately and support future coral conservation and restoration efforts.

‘Big call’ – Journalists react as Iraola names ‘exciting line-up’ for Liverpool in Istanbul

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Andoni Iraola has made five changes to the Liverpool starting XI for tonight’s Champions League clash against Fenerbahce, with the head coach not afraid to drop some big names to the substitutes’ bench.

There’s a signficant reshuffle in attack as Florian Wirtz, Cody Gakpo and Alexander Isak are all taken out of the line-up, with Dominik Szoboszlai, Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz coming in to start.

The Reds have no recognised centre-forward in their starting XI, so it looks likely that Bradley Barcola will move infield to lead the line.

When the team news was announced 75 minutes before kick-off, two Liverpool Echo reporters covering the game in Istanbul gave their immediate takes to some eye-catching selections by Iraola.

Paul Gorst wrote on the live matchday blog: ‘No recognised striker for Liverpool, so my hunch is it is Bradley Barcola who will lead the line tonight. It’s an exciting line-up, though. Munoz, Ngumoha, Szoboszlai all in there alongside Barcola.’

Ian Doyle focused on Trey Nyoni keeping his place alongside Alexis Mac Allister, saying: ‘Nyoni starts a third successive game, which is a big call. Not on form, but in terms of fitness for a youngster.’

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

A relentless fixture list and a quick turnaround from the defeat to Arsenal on Sunday has no doubt been a factor in Iraola making so many changes, but it’s by no means a weak starting XI for Liverpool.

The full-back combination of Joe Gomez and Kostas Tsimikas may seem surprising, but both are experienced campaigners who won’t wilt in the intense atmosphere of the Sukru Saracoglu Stadium.

The decision to persist with Nyoni is a tremendous show of faith in the teenager, whose recent performances suggest that he might’ve kept his place even if Ryan Gravenberch hadn’t been missing through injury.

Iraola hasn’t been afraid to roll the dice, which is an ideal trait for a head coach to have. He’s made some big calls tonight, and hopefully those will pay off and our 100% record in the Champions League will be preserved going into matchday five later this month.

Add Cowboy Candy To Your Next Grilled Cheese For Triple The Flavor

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Making the best grilled cheese can be serious business. And though the modern grilled cheese didn't take off until the 1920s — when commercially sliced bread and process cheese made it an affordable household staple — one perfect add-on for your next sandwich carries a classic frontier spirit. It's called cowboy candy — sugared and pickled jalapeño peppers — and each slice adds an incredible amount of flavor to your meal.

If you like the sweet and sour mix of bread and butter pickles, and you can handle a decent hit of spiciness, you'll love cowboy candy. When jalapeños are pickled, the brine can mellow their heat, so you won't just get a blast of intense spiciness. You'll get crunchy, vegetal, and herbaceous notes; some nice tang from the vinegar; plus a hit of sweetness. And it goes with just about any cheese you want to put on your sando. Cheddar or Monterey Jack are classic American options. If you want more nuttiness, you can try Gruyère, or give Havarti a go for some nice buttery flavor that would also be delicious with caramelized onions. And adding some spicy chorizo will make it a full meal.

The history of cowboy candy begins in the Lone Star State. WHH Ranch, which is based in Texas, claims to be the originator of the condiment and the oldest family-owned cannery in the state. These days, you can order a variety of cowboy candy versions from its website.

If you want to make cowboy candy at home, it's not hard to do on your own. You're going to need a mix of sugar, vinegar, and pickling spices. The mixture will cook down into a thick syrup to give the peppers a nice sugary coating that turns a regular spicy grilled cheese into a masterful mix of flavors. For the spices, you can go the standard route with mustard seeds, celery seeds, and turmeric for the tasty bread and butter pickle style. But a little dried or fresh dill will get you closer to a classic dill pickle flavor, and some chipotle pepper powder will add more smokiness. If you want more of a pungent tang, you can add some apple cider vinegar.

When you're making your cowboy candy grilled cheese, think about what cheeses will blend best with the sweet, spicy, savory flavor. Maybe try a nice sourdough or thick Texas toast, and don't be afraid to try some other protein additions. Turn it into a taco-inspired sandwich with some seasoned ground beef, or use up the leftovers from your rotisserie chicken. Just get that cowboy candy for your next grilled cheese, and from there, you can pile on the flavor.

PS5 Exclusive God of War Laufey Has Officially Been Delayed by Three Months

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Developer Santa Monica Studio has just announced its upcoming game, God of War Laufey, has been delayed.

Originally planned for release on 16th February 2027, the PS5 exclusive action game will now launch just over three months later on 20th May.

The news comes via a statement published to social media:

"Since the reveal of God of War Laufey in June, we've been deeply grateful to see so much excitement for Faye's journey through the Everywhen," the statement says. "We don't take for granted the trust placed in our studio to make an extraordinary experience worthy of the passion and dedication of our fans."

Santa Monica Studio says it'll "make the most of each moment until then by putting in the finishing touches and pushing the game to its fullest potential."

God of War Laufey is the next entry in Sony's popular action series, chronicling the journey of Kratos' late wife, Faye, as she navigates the afterlife.

As mentioned in the above statement, the game was officially announced in June of this year, and even then it appeared to be close to release. At the time, some thought Sony was pushing Laufey harder than the imminent Marvel's Wolverine.

Still, the game will launch within a year of its announcement, which is pretty good going.
