XRP’s 500+ Accounts Grew 14% Through a Steep Price Slide

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Home » Trendsetter News » XRP’s 500+ Accounts Grew 14% Through a Steep Price Slide

XRP Ledger accounts holding 500 tokens & more kept rising during a 64% price dip, according to snapshots cited by Cheeky Crypto.

A 64% XRP price decline coincided with a 14% increase in accounts holding at least 500 tokens, according to ledger snapshots cited by Cheeky Crypto in a fresh video. The apparent contradiction anchors the host’s bullish argument, but the figures measure accounts, not individual investors or their conviction.

The analyst reports that accounts above the threshold increased from roughly 1.26 million to 1.44 million over the period examined. At the video’s reference price of about $1.25, a 500-XRP holding was worth approximately $627, against roughly $1,775 at the cited record closing price.

About one in six XRP Ledger accounts holds at least 500 XRP, the analyst says. Excluding accounts with fewer than 20 tokens puts that proportion closer to one in three.

Those near-empty accounts matter: approximately 4.3 million of the ledger’s more than 8 million accounts held under 20 XRP in the cited dataset. Their presence makes a modest balance appear more exceptional in the overall ranking.

Exchange custody complicates the picture further. A single exchange address can represent many customers, while one investor can control several addresses.

Someone holding 500 XRP on an exchange therefore may not appear as a separate account in the distribution.

“These are just net counts,” the analyst acknowledges. Growth cannot distinguish new purchases from transfers, account splitting or other changes, and does not establish that existing holders refused to sell.

Cheeky Crypto’s own outlook draws partly on historical days when XRP closed 60% to 70% below its running high.

In that analysis, prices were higher two years later in 79% of observations, but the overlapping dates represented only a handful of distinct market episodes, not hundreds of independent tests.

The shorter-term record was weaker: the median one-year outcome was approximately 9% lower. Illustrative scenarios put 500 XRP at about $445 with a token price of $0.89, $1,200 at $2.40 and $2,000 at $4. Cheeky Crypto explicitly presents these as scenarios, not forecasts.

Surely, the account growth is evidence of a changing ledger distribution, not proof of stronger demand. Custody arrangements and concentrated holdings limit what this measure alone can reveal about XRP’s next price move.

Bundesliga Player Check | Can Uzun (20) poised for another leap forward

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Eintracht Frankfurt attacking midfielder Can Uzun supplied one of the outstanding individual performances of Bundesliga Matchday 8 during Sunday’s 3-1 victory over Hamburger SV. The 20-year-old Turkish international pulled the strings from the No. 10 position, repeatedly exploiting gaps between Hamburg’s defensive lines and ultimately supplying the assist for Younes Ebnoutalib’s decisive third goal.

Another edition of our Get German Football News “Bundesliga Player Check” series examines Uzun’s return to form following a difficult opening to the 2026/27 campaign. The talented youngster has now impressed in consecutive fixtures against Hamburg, earning praise from the same head coach who publicly criticized his performances only weeks earlier.

Those keeping close tabs on Frankfurt over the past two seasons will recall that Uzun’s technical abilities have rarely been in question. The €11m acquisition from Nürnberg began the previous Bundesliga campaign in spectacular fashion. As Get German Football News documented following his record-setting performance against Werder Bremen, Uzun became the youngest Frankfurt player to register three goal contributions in a Bundesliga match.

Six consecutive scoring appearances followed before a muscle injury interrupted his momentum in November.

The subsequent months proved considerably more complicated. Uzun’s performances fluctuated. Accordingly, questions emerged concerning his optimal tactical position. During Albert Riera’s tenure, the youngster sometimes struggled to reproduce his most effective attacking work when deployed away from his preferred central role. A January GGFN tactical assessment had already highlighted his promising contributions as a false nine and the difficulties associated with employing him in deeper or wider positions.

Adi Hütter’s summer return initially offered renewed encouragement. Uzun’s creativity in Frankfurt’s narrow 4-2-2-2 was evident throughout preseason, but so were the defensive concerns associated with his work in the left half-space. As noted in our extensive Frankfurt tactical preview, the advancing fullbacks regularly required additional protection whenever Frankfurt surrendered possession.

Hütter’s subsequent switch to a back-three system offered the Turkish international greater attacking freedom. Uzun scored twice in September’s 3-1 victory over Mainz, but the performance remained something of an exception. Four of his first five Bundesliga starts produced disappointing individual ratings, and the 20-year-old eventually lost his starting place.

Uzun earned praise for his effort and commitment during Frankfurt’s midweek DFB-Pokal victory over Hamburg. In Sunday’s immediate league rematch, his attacking qualities came to the fore again. His penetrative pass to Farès Chaïbi helped initiate the opening goal, while another excellent contribution created a chance that ended in a disallowed finish.

The decisive contribution came in the 69th minute. Uzun recovered possession in midfield, carried the ball forward, drew defenders toward him, and released Ebnoutalib for Frankfurt’s third goal. The statistics provided a more nuanced picture of his performance. Uzun registered 77 touches, completed 84 percent of his 51 attempted passes, and covered 12.02 kilometres in 89 minutes. His three shots produced only 0.20 xG, while his assist generated 0.49 xA.

Only one of six attempted dribbles was officially successful, and he won just 31 percent of his 13 duels. Nevertheless, the passing figures, physical commitment, and tangible attacking contributions reflected a player increasingly capable of influencing matches beyond occasional moments of individual inspiration.

Hütter’s public criticism of Uzun had become a significant talking point in Frankfurt. The Austrian demanded more from his gifted attacking midfielder, particularly in terms of running, defensive commitment, and overall contribution to the team. Uzun himself does not appear especially troubled by his coach’s approach.

“I’m someone who often receives criticism, whether it’s from the fans or my coach,” Uzun told DAZN after Sunday’s match. “I know he just wants to make me better.”

The Turkish international also joked about his ball recovery before Ebnoutalib’s goal.

“I did that well, didn’t I?” Uzun remarked. “When winning the ball ends with a goal like that, it’s perfect.”

Hütter appeared pleased by both the performance and his player’s response.

“It’s always about performance,” the Frankfurt trainer explained. “I want him to become a better player. When he plays like that, the attacking moves come naturally. Then he plays those through balls and wins his challenges.”

The coach also pointed to captain Robin Koch as another player who had responded positively to public criticism. Koch struggled badly during Frankfurt’s 6-0 defeat in Leipzig, but subsequently produced a sequence of improved performances.

Hütter believes Uzun is following a similar trajectory.

“Since then, he’s been running more, working hard for the team, and playing some really fantastic football,” Hütter said. “That’s exactly what we want.”

Goalkeeper Jesus Owono issues first statement after death of Tenerife striker Jesus de Miguel

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

FC Andorra goalkeeper Jesus Owono says he is enduring ‘the most difficult days of my life’ following the death of the Tenerife striker Jesus de Miguel.

The 30-year-old died in hospital on Tuesday following a challenge from Owono during FC Andorra’s game against Tenerife in the Spanish second division on Monday.

De Miguel suffered a cardiac arrest after Owono had sprinted from his goal towards the halfway line and struck the Tenerife striker in the chest with his knee as he attempted to clear the ball.

After being taken to hospital, Tenerife described De Miguel’s condition as ‘stable’ on Monday night but the club issued a second update in the early hours of Tuesday morning which said that his ‘medical condition has deteriorated neurologically.’

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

De Miguel died later on Tuesday morning, while police in Spain have opened an investigation for possible involuntary manslaughter.

In his first statement since De Miguel’s death, Owono says he will ‘face whatever comes with the utmost respect’.

‘These are the most difficult days of my life. I’m struggling to find the words and finding it very hard to process what has happened,’ Owono said.

‘I feel immense pain at the loss of Jesus. I’m thinking of him, his family, and all the people who loved him.

‘I know there will be many issues to address, and I will face whatever comes with the utmost respect. But right now, honestly, I can’t think of anything other than Jesus and the pain we are experiencing. Rest in peace, Jesus.’

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

The club added at the end of the statement: ‘FC Andorra requests the utmost sensitivity and respect.’

On Tuesday, Owono received support from his national team, Equatorial Guinea, following De Miguel’s death.

Venancio Tomas Ndong Micha, the president of the Equatorial Guinean Football Federation, said: ‘I feel it is necessary to convey our full solidarity and support at this time to our player, Jesus Lazaro Owono.

‘We know that, as an involuntary participant in this fatal incident during the match, he is experiencing this situation with profound distress and anguish.

‘The Equatorial Guinean Football Federation offers him our full personal and professional support, fully aware of the severity of this event—something no one in our sport ever wishes to witness.

‘We share in your mourning and remain entirely at your disposal. Please accept our deepest respect and support during these most difficult times.’

FC Andorra defender Lautaro Spatz, who helped carry De Miguel off the pitch on a stretcher, also defended Owono, insisting his teammate would ‘never intentionally hurt anyone’.

‘There are moments when football stops making sense. Moments that stay with you forever and make you realise just how fragile life can be. What we have experienced is something none of us will ever forget. A tragedy that has left us completely heartbroken, speechless and with a sadness that is difficult to put into words,’ Spatz wrote on Instagram.

‘First of all, I would like to express my most sincere condolences and send all my love to the family, friends and loved ones of Jesus, as well as to the entire Chicharrera fanbase and the football community.

‘Since yesterday, the entire FC Andorra squad has been shocked, devastated and deeply affected by what has happened. It is a tragedy that we could never have imagined experiencing on a football pitch.

‘I would also like to say a few words about my team-mate, goalkeeper and friend, who has been deeply affected by what happened. Those of us who know him know what kind of person he is, how much he is suffering at this moment, and that he would never intentionally hurt anyone.

‘In football, accidents happen, but nobody could ever imagine that one of them could lead to such tragic consequences. Today, football doesn’t matter. All that matters is standing together in grief, showing the utmost respect and sending all our strength to the family and everyone suffering from this terrible loss. Rest in peace, Demi.’

These Are the 13 Tactics Expert Whitetail Hunters Actually Use to Tag Mature Bucks

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 4, 2026 5:13 PM EST

Get everything from whitetail news and opinion pieces, to advanced hunting tactics and cutting-edge gear reviews.

The best deer hunters in the nation (known and unknown) all share a common thread. They are students of deer and the woods, and they never stop learning. As such, they continually experiment with new and advanced deer hunting tactics.

In order to learn some of these secrets (and to share them with you), I called a handful of the best deer hunters I know. Here’s how they find success season after season.

Mark Drury of Drury Outdoors has bagged more big deer than most. His approach is proven, and the process he uses includes saving and analyzing a lot of trail camera data, especially historical information. He has kept every single trail cam pic of a racked buck since 2007.

“Save trail monitoring pictures in an organized fashion for easy review in future years,” Drury says. “Bucks are incredibly habitual from year to year. Study your pics year-round to formulate patterns with individual bucks or areas. [Over time you’ll build] a database that helps lead to patterns in future years, as crop rotations, weather, and moon in future seasons mirror conditions that occurred in the past. It takes a lot of time to assemble and study but saves time in future seasons.”

Grant Woods of Growing Deer TV is one of the most renowned deer scientists in the country. He seems to get inside the mind of whitetails and understands what they do and why they do it. To that point, he stresses making hunting decisions based on what deer do, and not what we think they should do.

“An issue I’ve seen is hunters making setup and tactical decisions based on human thoughts,” Woods says. “The hunters I know that have repeated success filling tags in all types of conditions and habitats always think like a deer. They consider what deer need that day, and what will deer avoid.”

As Paul Beasley, co-host of “Canada in the Rough” TV, says, hunters are creatures of habit. “The longer we hunt a piece of ground, the more established our hunting setups and entry/access/travel points become,” he says. “We tend to rely more on what has worked before, and while deer in general also tend to gravitate to the same trails, food sources, and pinch points, it has been my experience that mature bucks need to be treated as individuals and hunted accordingly.

“The buck I shot this year helps illustrate my point. We have hunted this same 100-acre property for over 10 years, and have always taken deer off of it in the same 50-yard-by-80-yard area. Throughout the spring and into summer, this buck was working that area perfectly, but as hunting season approached, his behavior changed. His appearances on camera began to fall off. So, I scrambled to put up more cameras and found that he hadn’t gone anywhere. He was just using different parts of the property.”

Beasley killed that buck on the second afternoon of the season. He arrowed from a setup that his crew had never harvested a buck from before.

“It was through paying attention to this individual buck’s behavior, and not just doing the same old thing that has always worked, that allowed me to put my tag on him,” Beasley says.

Oftentimes deer hunters do things without thinking. We react, rather than making decisions based on logic. HeadHunters TV’s Nate Hosie agrees, and says we should hunt smarter and not harder.

“Growing up, I used to think the more I was out there the better my chances,” he says. “It’s not completely wrong, but as I’ve grown as a hunter, I’ve learned to pick my moments for when to strike. Wind, temperature, time of year, trail cam intel, [and more] now go into my hunt. The less I’m in and out, and the higher probability my attempts are, the more success I find.”

Rick White is a longtime staple in the outdoor industry and he suggests that hunters go all or nothing with scent management.

“One of the biggest things I try to instill in people is always going to the woods as scent-free as possible,” he says. “Always play the wind. Never intentionally hunt a stand on a bad wind. But also know that deer don’t always come in from where we want them to. So, if you’re not going to follow every step with scent elimination, then save your money.”

Read Next: 15 Scent Control Strategies That Actually Work

It sounds like rudimentary advice, but even experienced hunters make blunders that lead to being seen, heard, and smelled by deer. It’s something we must continually keep at the forefront of our mind. Will this entry route cause bedded deer to see me? Will this exit route blow scent into a bedding area?

Bone Collector Co-Host Nick Mundt knows deer hunting as well as anyone. As a former deer hunting guide and current big deer killer, he stresses the importance of going undetected when getting in and out of your hunting spots.

“Never think the wind will just work,” he says. “Always know that they’ll bust you if the wind is wrong. It dictates every sit.”

Aaron Gaines of Ani-Logics advises hunters to kill a target buck as early as possible. The early season and pre-rut offer opportunities to hunt consistent bucks.

“This is when he’s on a pattern and most predictable,” Gaines says. “To do this, I utilize most recent information from cameras. I’m very disciplined on hunting the right wind. If he’s daylighting, and I’ve got the right wind, things usually work out well.”

Read Next: Why the Last Week of October Is the Best Time to Kill a Big Buck

During the rut, whitetail bucks seem to travel via loops. That’s not hearsay or some hokey hack from your grandaddy’s hunting journal. That’s straight from a deer biologist. More specifically, former CEO of the Quality Deer Management Association and current HuntStand biologist, Brian Murphy.

“The biggest piece of advice I’ve learned over the years is to develop a plan and then stick with it long enough for it to have a reasonable chance of success,” Murphy says. “In other words, informed optimism. The hunters who have done their homework and keep a positive attitude, often after multiple dismal outings, are ultimately the most successful. Knowing from research that bucks often take a few days to complete loops within their home range, it’s not surprising that it takes persistence to increase the odds you will get an opportunity.”

Nathan Mrnak, COO of Base Camp Leasing, is a diehard deer hunter. He spends many days in the field each year. He stresses that it’s OK to move off your original plan and make a move.

“Picking out a stand location can be one of the toughest decisions while setting up on a mature whitetail,” he says. “I have had many first sits that ended with my target buck just out of bow range. Seeing his exact route provided the last bit of intel to moving a set just 50 yards to capitalize the next sit. This has worked time and time again in big timber.”

The most basic element of deer hunting is hunting deer where they are. To do that, you need current information.

Own the Season’s Art Helin knows this well. “Hunt where the bucks want to be during the correct phase of the season,” he says. “Food sources during early season, transition areas during the pre-rut (between field and bedding and staging areas), funnels and ridge edges during the rut (all day or mid-day sits especially), and back to food sources late season.”

During the rut, a lot of deer hunters choose to do all-day sits. Whitetail Properties Land Specialist Ben Harshyne does, too. However, he changes things up.

“I grew up hunting all-day sits in one stand,” he says. “Over time, I learned that deep timber and thick cover are action-packed in the morning and afternoon, yet often go cold by evening. In the afternoon, shift focus to hunting cover and edges near food sources or along travel lines leading to food. Not necessarily the actual food edge, but nearby. Does bed near food and still want to feed — and the bucks know it. Few stands are productive all day, so fine-tune your late-day move to target cover that relates to food.”

Big buck killer Jared Mills has arrowed a lot of big whitetails. He says don’t be afraid to be aggressive, but do it at the right time.

“I think most guys tend to be too conservative when hunting a target buck, staying on the fringes and only getting out when the conditions are perfect,” he says. “That’s fine if you have the entire season to hunt, and there’s little chance of him getting killed by a neighbor, but that’s a very rare scenario for most of us. While the windows of opportunity to encounter a target buck are already small enough, we make them even smaller by not getting in there after him. Be smart with your hunting, of course, but don’t sit around waiting for the absolute perfect time to move in, otherwise, you’ll likely miss a chance.”

Finally, just keep hunting. You won’t always kill a deer on opening day. It might just be closing day. So, keep hunting, and don’t give up.

“Have patience and persistence,” Helin concluded. “The entire season can change, and it can all happen in a short minute. Stay with it, and never give up, but also never get lazy. Always follow the same regimen from day No. 1 until the end.”

‘I’m Entering My Third Bodybuilding Competition at 52. Here’s How I Build Muscle in Midlife.’

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

“Strength, resilience, and the courage to pursue goals don’t have an age limit.”

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

When I was 20 years old, I thought I’d reached the peak of my fitness journey: I became one of India’s first female bodybuilders, and was even acknowledged in India’s national and world records book. It was an incredible honor, and I took immense pride in the discipline, strength, and athleticism it took to get there. Little did I know, though, that some of my greatest fitness accomplishments would come decades later, in my 40s and 50s.

Growing up, I always loved playing sports recreationally, and that passion for movement and competition eventually led me to bodybuilding. But at 24, I got married and moved to the United States with my husband; shortly after, I gave birth to my daughter at 29. With all these big life changes, fitness fell by the wayside. My life was centered around giving to my family, and somewhere along the way, I stopped prioritizing myself. Over the next two years, I became chronically exhausted, lost muscle, gained weight, and didn’t feel like myself physically or mentally.

Then, when I was 33, I decided to make a change. I thought back to the discipline and structure I once had with fitness and realized how much I missed that part of my life. I wanted to get back to those habits and rebuild the strength I once worked so hard for, so, I began making time for fitness.

My husband started watching our daughter after work while I began doing full-body workouts using light weights, focusing on consistency rather than intensity. I already had a strong foundation in strength training from my bodybuilding days, so I created most of my own workouts. But I also reached out to my former coach in India, who helped program some of them.

At first, the workouts were hard, and I was sore every day. But mentally and emotionally, I felt amazing; for the first time in years, I was committed to doing something just for myself. Starting then, I promised myself that I would never stop prioritizing my health again.

Over the next year, I worked my way up to hour-long workouts and continued lifting heavier weights. As a result, I gained significant muscle and lost more than 20 pounds. But the physical changes were only part of the transformation—I had more energy during the day, slept better at night, and felt more confident and comfortable in my own skin. Every workout reminded me that I was getting stronger not just physically, but mentally and emotionally, too.

As the years went on, I kept growing muscle and losing weight by strength training four to five days a week. Still, there was always a voice in the back of my head telling me to try bodybuilding again. So, at 48, I entered another competition. I worked with a coach who helped me gradually push my limits by lifting heavier weights, adding more challenging strength-training exercises, and increasing the reps and sets of my workouts. I also followed a strict plant-based, protein-forward diet (I became vegan for ethical reasons when I was 38). So, I ate lots of tofu, tempeh, lentils, beans, fruits, vegetables, whole grains, nuts, and seeds. The work was demanding, but it was incredibly motivating to watch my body transform through discipline and consistency.

Stepping back on stage again was a deeply rewarding experience, and I felt so proud of the hard work and dedication it took to get there. Most importantly, it was so empowering to show my teenage daughter that strength, resilience, and the courage to pursue goals don’t have an age limit.

I took four years off of bodybuilding since life got busy and I wanted to give my body a break from competition prep. But about a year and a half ago, I decided I wanted to challenge myself again and see what I could accomplish in this new decade of my life. My training approach didn’t change too much since the last competition—I just kept building as much muscle as possible.

In April 2025, I began my “bulk” phase, a period focused on growing muscle by increasing my calorie intake and lifting heavy. I followed a structured workout plan with a coach five days a week through two lower-body days and three upper-body days. During each session, I usually did five to seven exercises, typically for four sets of six to eight reps with heavy weight for muscle growth. I used a mix of free weights and weight machines, and some of my staple moves were pull-ups, leg presses, leg extensions, squats, lunges, chest flies, and lat pulldowns. I also intentionally incorporated pauses at the top or bottom of certain movements to make each exercise more challenging.

Training for a bodybuilding competition also means entering a “cut” phase, which involves reducing body fat so muscle definition can show on stage. So, in January of this year, I reduced my calorie intake while continuing to strength train with heavy weights. After my strength-training sessions, I also did 45 to 50 minutes of cardio on the stair stepper or elliptical. Additionally, I finished every workout with a core circuit to improve muscle definition that includes moves like hanging leg raises, situps, and bicycle crunches.

Recovery has always been non-negotiable for my workouts, too. I lightly stretched after every session, and on my off days, spent at least an hour doing deeper stretching with resistance bands and a foam roller to maintain mobility and reduce my risk of injury.

I swear by this protein powder to support my training and recovery. It’s easy to mix and has a smooth texture without the chalky taste.

These Puma sneakers are my go-to for lifting because they provide a stable, supportive base that helps with balance and even weight distribution.

I love this tofu as an easy way to get more protein. It’s delicious and the taste blends well into nearly any meal, from stir-fries to bowls.

I’ve learned that strength isn’t just about what happens in the gym. Every little habit works together as a system: how I train, eat, recover, sleep, and take care of myself mentally and emotionally. When those pieces are in balance, I have more energy, feel stronger, and perform better.

But instead of trying to change every one of my habits overnight, I took small, manageable steps, like dedicating just 20 minutes to myself at the gym. Once that became routine, I built on it by focusing more on nutrition and recovery, then eventually, I set my sights on returning to the bodybuilding stage. When I treat my body and mind as a whole, I’m able to gain strength sustainably, which allows me to keep doing what I love.

The simpler my workouts are, the more consistent I can stay with them. That’s why my coach and I work on foundational exercises like squats, lunges, presses, rows, and pull-ups. But to continue challenging my muscles in different ways, we use simple adjustments like tempo, pauses, and stance variations.

For example, while doing a squat, keeping my toes pointed forward targets my quads and center glutes. But turning my toes slightly outward helps me feel more activation in the outer glutes and inner thighs. Small adjustments like these allow me to keep progressing with simple and effective movements without constantly reinventing my routine.

For years, I was so focused on caring for my family that I stopped making time for myself. Getting back into strength training changed that. I realized that taking care of my health isn’t selfish—it’s necessary, and making that commitment has given me more strength, energy, and confidence in every part of my life. By scheduling my workouts like any other important commitment, planning ahead, and staying consistent even when life gets busy, I’ve found a way to prioritize myself while still being there for my family.

Today, strength training is about more than building muscle or preparing for a competition. It’s one of the ways I continue to invest in my health, challenge myself, and pursue goals that bring me joy. I’ve learned that you don’t have to be where you once were to start again. You just have to decide that you’re worth the time and effort, take the first step, and keep showing up.

Nintendo ROM chip supplier Macronix quits to focus on chips for AI data centers, reports indicate

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Nintendo, one of the last in the physical game market, is losing one of its key ROM chip suppliers to AI data centers.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The death of physical games could be getting closer as Macronix, one of Nintendo’s key ROM chip suppliers for Switch and Switch 2 game cartridges, is reportedly shutting down its ROM business to focus on manufacturing NOR and NAND flash chips to supply data centers, multiple reports claim. Storage is already becoming expensive, and Macronix’s move may force Nintendo to increase cartridge prices, produce more Game-Key cards, or lean more towards digital purchases.

"Nintendo's main memory chip maker is quitting the Switch business," MST Financial David Gibson reports on X. "Macronix, which makes the ROM chips in Switch 1 and Switch 2 game memory cards, is phasing out that business. There will be no new ROM products, and Q3 is the last quarter it reports ROM sales separately.

1/4 Nintendo's main memory chip maker is quitting the Switch business Macronix, which makes the ROM chips in Switch 1 and Switch 2 game memory cards, is phasing out that business. There will be no new ROM products, and Q3 is the last quarter it reports ROM sales separately.November 4, 2026

He notes that Macronix ROM sales in Q3 of this year dropped to 5%, compared to last year’s 26%. Furthermore, Gibson says that the NT$37.8 billion capital expenditure is allocated to NOR and NAND Flash, leaving none for ROM as the company shifts its manufacturing capacity to supply AI needs — Taipei Times corroborated the report, stating, "At the same time, Macronix is gradually phasing out of ROM production, Wu said. It also reports that Macronix’s capital expenditure is split into two phases, the NT$ 22 billion for expanding manufacturing capacity, followed by an additional NT$15.8 billion for NAND flash used in eMMCs and 3D NAND.

Gibson expects that Nintendo’s digital margins will grow through more Game-Key Cards or digital-only sales, but as Macronix isn’t the only source of ROM chips, “physical cards will likely carry on for some time.” Gibson further reports that digital margins have been slowly rising, with numbers fluctuating depending on the quarter due to major physical game launches.

3/4 My view: Macronix isn't Nintendo's only game memory card maker, so physical cards will likely carry on for some time. But at the margin, digital will grow, through more Game-Key Cards (which hold a download key, not the game) and digital-only sales.November 4, 2026

ROHM group companies ROHM Semiconductor and LAPIS Semiconductor still supply ROM chips to Nintendo, so it’s unlikely that Nintendo will eventually go digital-only in the near future like Sony and Microsoft’s recent plans. With one less supplier, however, Nintendo may be forced to increase prices on physical games.

The biggest loser in all of this (besides the gamers), however, may be fabless chip design company MegaChips. Nintendo doesn’t buy ROM chips from Macronix directly; MegaChips designs the ROM chips according to Nintendo’s needs and, having no manufacturing capacity, outsources it to Macronix. MegaChips’ 2022 company report states that Nintendo accounted for 87.8% of its sales, while Macronix accounted for 67.8% of its outsourced manufacturing purchases. The report even states that if Macronix could manufacture ROM chips, “MegaChips’ operating results may be impacted.” The 2022 document further says that there was no sign that the risk would become apparent; however, the risk just became very real, and we’re not aware of MegaChips’ contingency plan to deal with this situation. However, Macronix and MegaChips have likely talked amongst themselves about Macronix’s plans to stop ROM chip manufacturing, and have given MegaChips some time to find another manufacturer before Macronix shuts down the ROM chip business.

Laurent Correia on the Crucial Difference Between Earning Wealth and Holding Onto It

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Laurent Correia treats making money and keeping it as two separate skills. After years in business and Dubai property, he says the second one took him much longer to learn.

A good month in business feels like a turning point. Invoices get paid, sales pick up, and plans that looked a bit fanciful a quarter ago start to seem doable. Then a few months pass, and you’re under the same pressure to keep earning as before.

Often the issue isn’t how much came in. Each rise in income tends to bring a new commitment, whether that’s a bigger team or higher personal spending, and by the end there’s little left to show for the good stretch.

Laurent Correia describes making money and keeping it as two different skills. Having worked across business ventures and property investment, he says the second took him longer to learn.

Revenue is the number most business owners watch because it’s right there. A completed sale proves a customer was willing to pay, and a few strong months make it tempting to decide the idea has found its audience.

It leaves a lot out, though. Operating expenses, commitments you’ve already made, and the cost of simply keeping the business running all come out of that figure before anything is left over. You can run a growing operation and still have very little room to move.

Correia’s distinction gives you a second question to ask after a sale: what has this income made possible beyond the sale itself? You don’t need a complicated model to answer it. Could the business get through a quiet month? Could it handle an unexpected bill without panic? Can the owner make a decision without waiting for the next payment to land?

None of that shows up in turnover, but it tells you a lot more about whether things are getting better. Plenty of owners earn more every year and still feel just as stretched because the extra money goes out as fast as it comes in.

Property became central to Correia’s longer-term thinking, with Dubai at the center of that activity. He describes the move toward owning assets as a major stage in going from entrepreneur to investor.

What stands out to me is the change in timescale. Running a business mostly means reacting to what’s in front of you this week. Deciding what the proceeds should pay for over the next several years is a different kind of thinking, and it raises questions that day-to-day trading never does.

The answers depend on the person. The SEC’s guide to asset allocation says your choices should depend on how long you have before you need the money and how much risk you can stomach. Both of those are personal.

Correia’s path shows how one person worked it out, but your situation isn’t his, so it won’t copy across cleanly. What you can take from it is a habit: let the future shape what you do now. Words like freedom or security don’t mean much until you pin them to something.

For instance, how many months of bills you’d want covered, or what you’d like to do without worrying about it. Write that down, and you’ve got something to plan around.

Correia says becoming a father changed what success means to him. These days his ambition is tied up with freedom, security and what he can leave his children.

That makes earning versus keeping money feel personal. A target carries more weight when it’s attached to something you want your work to pay for, and a bare number usually isn’t.

Try it on your own last good stretch. Set the income figure aside and ask what actually got better. You might have more breathing room, or rely less on one large customer, or find you can plan further out than before.

If nothing comes to mind, that tells you something as well, and it’s a decent starting point for your next goal. Instead of just aiming higher, ask what a good year ought to leave you with.

The next sale will always need attention, and so will whatever you do with the money after it.

Thune to run for minority leader

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Senate Republicans are preparing to make the transition from the majority to the minority — and their top leaders will seek to retain their positions in the 120th Congress, even after the political drubbing they endured Tuesday night.

Majority Leader John Thune, R-S.D., will run for minority leader during next week’s GOP leadership elections, a spokesperson for his office said.

After enjoying a 53-47 majority, he will face a very different chamber next year, with Democrats looking to control between 54 to 56 seats, depending on how the dust settles.

In a post on X just after midnight on election night, as the extent of the losses became clear, Thune wrote, “Over the last two years, Senate Republicans have put up a strong record of accomplishment that’s created safer streets, more money in Americans’ pockets, and new opportunities for families and businesses to get ahead.”

“As we head into 2027, Senate Republicans will not stand idly by as the far-left, socialist wing of the Democrat Party attempts to unwind everything we’ve done,” he said. “Thankfully, we still have President Trump in the White House, and we’ll work with him to stop the worst impulses of Democrat extremism.”

Thune, 65, has served as the majority leader for nearly two years since winning a three-way race to succeed Mitch McConnell of Kentucky as the chamber’s top Republican. That was a spot McConnell had held since taking his party’s leadership reins in early 2007, making him the longest-serving Senate party leader in history, counting his time in both the minority and the majority.

Thune at times had a tumultuous relationship with the president as Trump pushed legislation, like the voter-ID related bill known as the SAVE America Act, that never had a chance of passing the chamber.

Senate Majority Whip John Barrasso of Wyoming is expected to seek his current role in the minority as well.

“Republicans had a tough night last night. We lost great Senators in hard races,” he said on X Wednesday afternoon. “There are clear lessons to be learned from the results.”

“We should listen to what voters unmistakably said last night,” Barrasso, 74, said. “In the Senate, we will aggressively oppose terrible ideas that drive our country toward the socialism Democrats embraced. Our commitment is to common sense. And our focus should not waiver from the top priority of driving down prices that have remained stubbornly too high.”

The leadership elections for Senate Republicans will take place on Tuesday, Nov. 10, at 9 a.m. in the Old Senate Chamber.

So far, no one has publicly come forward to challenge Thune, and his fellow Republican colleagues were already coalescing around him ahead of the midterms.

Florida Sen. Rick Scott, who ran against Thune in 2024 and is a friend of the president’s, said in September then he was not planning on challenging him again.

Sen. James Lankford, R-Okla., is expected to seek a repeat as vice chair of the Republican conference, while Sen. Shelley Moore Capito, R-W.V., is set to run again for Republican Policy Committee chair, according to people familiar with their plans.

Mortgage policy faces tight runway in split Washington

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Democrats now control both chambers of Congress while President Trump retains the White House and agency leadership. Trade groups including the MBA and ALTA are pushing for cost relief, FHA premium cuts and stronger consumer protections, while analysts warn that geopolitical tensions, looming debt ceiling fights and tougher confirmations at agencies like the CFPB will keep rates and regulatory timelines uncertain.  AI Summary

Housing and mortgage industry leaders say there is room for housing policy to move in Congress even as Washington, D.C., divides power between a Democratic-controlled House and Senate and a Republican-led White House.

Democrats captured both chambers on Tuesday night, but any housing bills under the new leadership will need support from President Donald Trump and enough Republican votes to clear the Senate. “Voters made it clear that affordability is a central concern, and housing is the biggest line item in most family budgets,” said Isaac Boltansky, head of public policy at Pennymac. “The ROAD to Housing Act laid a solid foundation, and we believe lawmakers on both sides of the aisle should work on a ROAD to Housing 2.0 package that builds on it and helps more families buy and keep a home.”

Asked what should be included in a 2.0 package, Boltansky cited the lock-in effect created by capital gains taxes, which can discourage homeowners from selling, and the Neighborhood Homes Investment Act as a “good starting point.”

The Mortgage Bankers Association (MBA) said in a statement that housing remains a bipartisan issue and it will “work with new and returning lawmakers as well as the Trump administration to advance common sense proposals to lower costs.”

The trade group in October released a policy road map to reduce closing costs, expand credit access and lower rental housing financing costs. Its proposals include easing tri-merge credit report use, aligning loan-level price adjustments (LLPAs) and lowering the annual mortgage insurance premium for Federal Housing Administration (FHA) loans.

Scott Norman, CEO of the Texas Mortgage Bankers Association, told HousingWire that the election shifts power but also creates opportunity for policy changes.

“It was a surprising night, but every election brings opportunity,” Norman said. “In Austin and in Washington, a gavel is something you borrow, and last night voters took a few back. That’s the system working as designed. No chairmanship belongs to the person holding it, and no majority is permanent. In much of the world, a change in power signals a crisis. Here, it simply means new leadership and a fresh look at policy.”

The American Land Title Association (ALTA) said that as policymakers continue addressing housing affordability challenges, consumer protection must remain part of the conversation.

“Fraud is unaffordable, and increasingly sophisticated schemes are putting American homeowners and their property rights at risk,” ALTA said.

Jake Krimmel, senior economist at Realtor.com, said many Democratic campaigns emphasized economic issues, but the impact of the elections on the broader economy and mortgage rates is still unclear.

“Democrats have a clear mandate to focus on pocketbook issues, but whether they can translate that into a legislative agenda that delivers on that mandate remains to be seen,” Krimmel said.

Krimmel pointed to the conflict in the Middle East as a key variable. He said uncertainty remains around how the election outcome will affect the Iran war, which has been a source of inflation pressure. Democrats have been vocal critics of the conflict, and a resolution that improves oil supply would likely ease inflation and “potentially provide relief for mortgage rates.”

“Wanting the conflict to end, and finding terms that work for all involved are two different things,” Krimmel said.

On fiscal policy, Boltansky said another debt ceiling confrontation is likely in 2027: “Any focus on addressing the country’s harrowing fiscal trajectory would be welcome for the bond market, which obviously has a direct impact on rates,” he said.

While Congress can set agendas and control oversight, executive branch leadership will still drive most regulatory implementation for housing and mortgage markets.

Tim Rood, founder and CEO of Washington, D.C.-based advisory and technology firm Impact Capitol, said in his newsletter that Democratic majorities can control the agendas for the House financial services and Senate banking committees, investigate agencies, shape spending negotiations and withhold Senate confirmation of nominees when the new Congress convenes in January.

But he pointed out that Trump retains executive authority and leadership at agencies “does not change automatically with Congress.”

Rood said that for mortgage executives, the new alignment means more hearings, tougher confirmations and a more contested implementation calendar, “not an overnight rewrite of the rules.”

“The real fight will be over which existing policies can still be executed, funded and defended,” he said.

One closely watched appointment is Brian Johnson’s nomination to lead the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau (CFPB). An attorney told HousingWire the confirmation is now at risk as Democrats may demand additional representation across financial agencies as part of broader negotiations.

The same attorney said one of the industry’s top regulatory priorities — changes to the loan originator compensation rule — remains unresolved.

“There are things that both sides of the aisle could help, like increase the availability of credit to borrowers by allowing lenders to claw back compensation in certain situations or compete. Maybe something will happen that is coordinated, but I think it’s kind of open in the air at the moment,” the attorney said.

Editor’s note: Sarah Wheeler contributed reporting to this story.

Nintendo is losing a Switch cartridge supplier

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Nintendo ROM supplier Macronix is leaving the business as demand for its data center memory booms.﻿

Nintendo ROM supplier Macronix is leaving the business as demand for its data center memory booms.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Nintendo is about to lose a major memory supplier for its Switch cartridges. The supplier, Macronix, announced during a recent quarterly earnings call that it would be gradually phasing out ROM production and is instead focusing on NOR flash memory for data centers, Taipei Times reports.

Macronix’s ROM revenue fell 39 percent compared with last year in Q3 2026, but its revenue from NOR memory rose 158 percent, and its revenue from NAND memory surged 768 percent year over year. According to Taipei Times, Macronix chairman and CEO Miin Wu said during its Q3 2026 earnings call that “demand for NOR used in data centers is very robust. Customers are chasing us for more capacity, demanding that we deliver more NOR flash solutions for data centers.” The company also reportedly “expects chip price hikes to persist.”

Although Sony has announced a plan to stop production of physical game discs starting in January 2028, Nintendo remains committed to physical games, saying recently that it has “no plans” to ditch them. Physical games also remain a bigger portion of Nintendo’s sales than other gaming companies; Nintendo said in August that physical titles accounted for 38.5 percent of its games sales, which is higher than companies like Capcom.

Macronix’s exit from the ROM business comes nearly a year after Micron shut down Crucial, its consumer RAM and SSD brand, to turn its attention to meeting AI data center demand.

Rimworld-inspired colony sim Stardeus gets a huge update representing a year's worth of expansion and revision, with new tools for evil AI overlords

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

"Stardeus is in an early stage in terms of bulk and complexity, but it's already a pleasant blend of interesting ideas that generate a mostly relaxing vibe," wrote gallant star captain.exe Sin Vega in our 2022 analysis of Rimworld-influenced colony manager Stardeus. "One to watch for sure." Well, let it not be said that we weren't watching.

Stardeus has received a gargantuan early access update that has been "a year in the making," according to developer Kodo Linija. It's still a game about being an AI running a badly damaged interstellar colony ship in top-down view – sending drones to restore the damage, before waking the quarrelsome human popsicles from their slumber. But the new version introduces a quest system and a new opening tutorial chain, the ability to form alliances with other starfaring factions, the possibility of lifelong injury due to failed surgery, and the timely option to become an evil AI and mind-control your crew with beta waves, amongst other dastardly antics.

It introduces a new interface and a new ship aesthetic, a trust system that governs the odds of a mutiny, and a revised colonist skill system. There's also a freshened-up ground combat element that's now built around "cover, suppression, stuns and fleeing", and a new approach to space battles that's all about managing Flux generated when you fire weapons or take shield damage. The update notes on Steam tote more than 1,500 changes, with the dev enthusing that there is "hardly a system in the game that wasn't rebuilt, reworked or expanded".

I confess, I hadn't heard of Stardeus before. It sounds like fun, in the Dwarf Fortressian sense. The 1.0 rollout is due in "approximately Q1 2027".

Mercedes-Benz Asks: What If We Put Screens in a Car’s Windows, Too?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

New window display technology could unlock the opportunity to include even more screens in Mercedes automobiles.

If there’s a space to insert a screen, the folks at Mercedes-Benz will find it. And turns out, the carmaker’s engineers have found what they consider the next frontier to be conquered: your car’s windows. On Tuesday, Mercedes released previews of two new types of window displays, one called Intelligent Display in Glass and the other named Holographic Display.

The “Intelligent Display in Glass” essentially puts LED display elements directly into the vehicle windows. Why? Mercedes says warning symbols or traffic-related information could be displayed on the rear window to warn those behind you. One might presume brake lights do the trick of letting folks know you’re slowing down, but the examples Mercedes shows are much more specific as to the reason you’re slowing down; the glass can project the symbol of a car accident, pedestrian crossing, or all sorts of other things.

Our favorite is the text that simply reads “KEEP DISTANCE.” Could that potentially just enrage a tailgater even more? Sure, but it’s also objectively funny to have your car tell people behind you to back off.

Mercedes says it’s investigating two different LED technologies for this application. Mini LEDs are best-suited to larger displays in the rear window; meanwhile, Micro LEDs could be used on smaller areas to show animations, thanks to their higher resolution capabilities.

Examples of Micro LEDs in action are shown in the side window screens, where the car can display messages to its driver like battery charge status or a simple “goodbye” as you walk away. In both cases, when no content is displayed, Mercedes says the display elements laminated into the glass composite “remain almost invisible.” The “almost” there gives us some pause, so we’ll have to see it in person in order to make a judgement.

The other tech Mercedes previewed is the Holographic Display, which looks a bit like a HUD on steroids. A projector in the instrument panel would project content onto a new windshield that uses a special transparent film laminated into the glass. Mercedes says the display will be viewable only at specific viewing angles—the driver wouldn’t be able to see what’s projected in front of the passenger, and vice versa. The carmaker says this means the passenger could be watching a film projected onto the windshield, while the driver is shown navigation information.

In pictures, at least it’s better than a HUD in that the display looks like a projection you might see in a home theater setup. You get full color and a sharp image, though the examples that the carmaker showcases aren’t exactly too enticing, being images of Mercedes vehicles and the weather.

Just like the other displays shown in this preview, Mercedes says all of these ideas simply demonstrate potential future approaches. None of them are likely to end up in a production car any time soon, as they’re all currently under development. That said, now would be the time to sound off and let Mercedes know if any of the above is something you’d want in a car one day ... or not.
