But Our Flag Was Still There

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Imagine a time when Americans could be arrested by soldiers and imprisoned without the ordinary opportunity to challenge their detention before a judge. When a former congressman could be tried by a military commission for a political speech, then banished from the country. When newspaper offices could be occupied by troops and publication stopped. Imagine an editor imprisoned in the very fortress his grandfather had immortalized in a song about American freedom.

O say, can you see By the dawn’s early light

I woke up this morning thinking about the question at the beginning of our national anthem.

After an election, we tend to look for answers. This morning, I find myself more interested in what we can still see.

I see a country in which people cast ballots and those ballots have consequences. A country in which officeholders can be challenged and voters can change the direction of government. I see people who care enough to argue, sometimes bitterly, about the future of a place they still believe is worth fighting for.

That is an exhausting way to govern ourselves, but what a remarkable inheritance!

What so proudly we hailed At the twilight’s last gleaming

The president in those opening examples was Abraham Lincoln.

His administration’s restrictions on civil liberties are part of our history, even if they sit uncomfortably alongside the achievements for which we rightly honor him.

Beginning in 1861, Lincoln authorized the suspension of habeas corpus, the means by which a detainee can ask a court to examine the legality of detention. The Constitution permits suspension in cases of rebellion or invasion when public safety requires it. Whether the president could exercise that authority without Congress was fiercely contested. The practical consequence was that people could be held by military authorities without the ordinary judicial protection against unlawful imprisonment.

In September 1862, his proclamation, circulated by the War Department as General Orders No. 141, subjected people discouraging enlistment or engaging in broadly described disloyal practices aiding the rebellion to military trial. It also suspended habeas corpus for those detained by military authority.

These were sweeping powers, with consequences far beyond the battlefield.

Whose broad stripes and bright stars Through the perilous fight

In 1863, a former Ohio congressman was arrested after delivering an antiwar speech, tried by a military commission, and convicted. Lincoln changed his punishment from imprisonment to banishment into Confederate territory.

Another critic, a Baltimore newspaper editor, had been arrested in 1861 and confined at Fort McHenry. His grandfather was Francis Scott Key. Think about that for a moment. The grandson of the man who wrote the words we sing about the land of the free became a political prisoner inside the fort associated with those words.

He was a Southern sympathizer. The former congressman’s politics were deeply objectionable in their defense of a slaveholding order. But we do not need to admire either man’s cause to take seriously the government’s treatment of him. Constitutional protections have little substance if they depend on our approval of the person invoking them.

O’er the ramparts we watched Were so gallantly streaming

General Ambrose Burnside ordered the suppression of the Chicago Times in 1863. Lincoln intervened to have that order revoked. That correction belongs in the account as much as the original abuse.

But in 1864, after the New York World and Journal of Commerce published a forged presidential proclamation, Lincoln himself ordered military action against the papers, including seizure of their printing establishments and the arrest of their editors and publishers.

The forgery was serious. So was the use of military power against newspapers.

All of this happened while the country was fighting a rebellion rooted in slavery, with its survival genuinely in doubt. That context is essential to understanding Lincoln’s decisions. Even so, it does not place those decisions beyond scrutiny and reminds us today there is nothing new under the sun.

I can regard Lincoln as a great president and still recoil at some of the powers exercised under his administration. An honest affection for America must leave room for that.

And the rockets’ red glare The bombs bursting in air

Our anthem was born of an actual bombardment. The danger was physical, and the outcome could not yet be known.

We sometimes speak of our own political moment as though we alone have discovered uncertainty. Every alarming development becomes unprecedented. Every election becomes the final opportunity to save the country.

Some warnings deserve our attention. Rights can erode, and institutions can fail. History offers plenty of evidence for the absolute necessity vigilance.

This country has been torn apart by civil war. It has tolerated profound injustice while congratulating itself on liberty. It has denied the protection of its promises to people who had every right to claim them.

And Americans have challenged those failures…often at great personal cost and against a majority quite comfortable with the way things were.

The work has never been easy, and progress has never been secure. The end of slavery did not prevent the injustices that followed. A constitutional promise could exist for generations before people received anything approaching its protection.

But the promise gave people something to demand. It still does.

Gave proof through the night That our flag was still there

I won’t comment on the outcome itself, but I have great hope after an election like this. The system is working. Imperfectly, unevenly, and with people understandably dissatisfied by its results. Yet democracy remains alive. Voters can register disapproval and impose consequences. Political power remains something that must answer to the public.

One election cannot certify the health of an entire republic. It can, however, demonstrate that the mechanisms of accountability still operate. I think we should allow ourselves to recognize that today, across political lines.

I spend much of my life traveling beyond our borders. That has made me more conscious of how much I value the ability to criticize my government openly and expect another opportunity to vote. Neither should be taken for granted.

We have built something very special here. Its failures are real, and so is its capacity for correction.

Repairing what is broken will require patient work long after the election coverage ends. Some damage will take years to undo. Some losses cannot be restored to the people who suffered them.

Still, Americans before us have undertaken that work under circumstances far more desperate than ours. We can honor them by continuing it.

This morning, I feel grateful that the country remains ours to shape. Grateful that disappointment can find expression at a ballot box. And I’m hopeful that we retain enough humility to recognize our mistakes and enough resolve to address them.

The anthem ends with a question. After all this time, it remains a question worth asking ourselves.

O say, does that star-spangled banner yet wave O’er the land of the free and the home of the brave

coperni closes: revisiting the fashion label’s strangest experiments in design and technology

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

from liquid textiles to garments embedded with microorganisms, coperni's experiments treated fashion as a platform for scientific research.

Coperni, the Paris fashion label that once sprayed a dress directly onto Bella Hadid in front of a live audience, has ceased operations. Founded by Sébastien Meyer and Arnaud Vaillant, the label spent the past few years turning some radical ideas into wearable objects.

Robot dogs walked alongside models, handbags were made from materials developed for space research, and a leather jacket became a functioning sound system. Much of this work involved collaborations with scientists and engineers, which brought onto the runway technologies rarely associated with fashion.

The news of Coperni’s closure, announced on November 4th, 2026, follows a dispute with its distributor and majority shareholder, Tomorrow. While the label’s future has come to an end, its experiments offer plenty worth revisiting. From liquid textiles that solidify on the body to clothing designed to interact with the skin’s microbiome, we look back at eight moments that made Coperni one of fashion’s most curious creative voices.

There can be no room for doubt: one of Coperni’s most recognizable moments came during its spring/summer 2023 show in Paris, when Bella Hadid stepped onto the runway to have a white dress sprayed directly onto her body. Developed with Fabrican, a material technology company founded by Spanish designer and scientist Manel Torres, the liquid contained fibers suspended in a solution that solidified into a flexible textile as it dried.

The process took place in front of the audience, with technicians gradually building the garment before a designer adjusted its neckline and cut a slit into the skirt. Within minutes, Hadid walked away wearing a fully formed dress.

Coperni Spring/Summer 23 Show | video via Coperni

For its fall/winter 2023 presentation, Coperni brought five Boston Dynamics Spot robots onto the runway to interact with models. Inspired by Jean de La Fontaine’s fable The Wolf and the Lamb, the performance imagined a possible relationship between humans and intelligent machines.

One robot gently removed a model’s jacket before returning it, while another briefly held onto Lila Moss’s handbag. The four-legged machines moved among the models and even approached the front row, turning the runway into an unusual encounter between fashion and robotics.

Coperni Fall/Winter 2023 Show | video via Coperni

For its fall/winter 2024 show, Coperni unveiled perhaps its most unusual material experiment: the Air Swipe Bag, developed with artist and researcher Ioannis Michaloudis. The translucent object was made from silica aerogel, an extremely lightweight solid consisting of approximately 99% air by volume and 1% glass-like material.

Associated with NASA’s research into capturing cosmic dust, aerogel gave the bag its pale, cloudlike appearance. Weighing around 33 grams, the sculptural accessory retained the recognizable Swipe silhouette despite its extraordinary material composition.

Air Swipe Bag by Coperni, made from 99% Air and 1% Glass | video via Coperni

The same collection introduced a different kind of wearable audio device: a 3D-printed handbag that doubled as a portable CD player. Based on Coperni’s curved Swipe silhouette, the metallic accessory accommodated a functioning disc player with wired headphones and a rechargeable battery.

Its appearance recalled the portable music devices of the 1990s, giving a familiar piece of consumer technology an unexpected second life as a fashion accessory.

For its spring/summer 2025 collection at Disneyland Paris, Coperni turned to an entirely different manufacturing process. Created with MIT spinout Rapid Liquid Print, the Ariel Swipe Bag was formed by extruding blue silicone into a bath of water-based gel.

The gel supported the material as a robotic nozzle drew the bag’s curved form, allowing the flexible object to take shape without conventional printed supports. Named after Disney’s mermaid character, the finished bag emerged from the gel with a soft, continuous surface.

Ariel Swipe Bag, Spring/Summer 2025 | video via Coperni

Coperni continued exploring the relationship between fashion and consumer technology in 2025 with its Tamagotchi Swipe Case, developed in collaboration with Japanese toy company Bandai. The miniature accessory adapted the brand’s recognizable handbag silhouette to accommodate a functioning digital pet, reviving the pocket-sized electronic toy that became popular in the late 1990s.

It arrived alongside Coperni’s fall/winter 2025 show, which took place inside a large-scale LAN party at Paris’s Adidas Arena, where hundreds of gamers played at computer stations while models moved between them.

Sound became a central theme of Coperni’s spring/summer 2024 collection, presented at IRCAM, the music and acoustics research institute in Paris. Working with Swedish audio company Transparent, the designers developed a leather jacket fitted with functioning speakers across its chest.

The engineering is surprisingly involved. Custom cavities were shaped to accommodate the audio components, with concealed wiring leading to an amplifier and battery pack stored in the jacket’s pockets. The finished garment allowed its wearer to carry a working sound system directly on the body.

One of the label’s final experiments moved into biotechnology. Released in October 2025, the C+ collection introduced close-fitting garments treated with probiotics and prebiotics, developed in collaboration with Swiss textile company HeiQ.

The fabric incorporates a bio-based matrix designed to release beneficial microorganisms through contact with the skin, activated by body heat and movement. The collection included leggings and bodysuits intended to support the skin’s microbiome during wear. It offered a different direction for Coperni’s interest in experimental design, imagining clothing as an active material that could interact with the body long after it left the runway.

STAT+: CVS Health to split, reversing years of deal-making to combine pharmacies and insurance

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Test your knowledge with our new weekday mini crossword

Bob covers the money in health care, focusing on health insurance and hospitals. His stories delve into Medicare Advantage, opaque prescription drug benefits, and how much executives actually make. He is also the author of the Health Care Inc. newsletter. You can reach Bob on Signal at bobjherman.09.

CVS Health will split into two separate companies, unraveling decades of deals that turned CVS into one of the largest health care conglomerates in the country.

Both of the new publicly traded companies will still have CVS in their names: CVS Health and CVS Pharmacy.

CVS Health will include the Caremark pharmacy benefit manager, the Aetna health insurer, the specialty pharmacy, and companies that provide primary care and home visits to older adults. That means high-cost drugs from the specialty pharmacy will still be intertwined with one of the country’s largest PBMs and insurers — a point of contention among critics who believe companies like CVS are profiting excessively from prescription drug dispensing.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time reviews are in, and critics agree on one thing

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Nintendo has worked its magic on a 28-year-old classic.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Early reviews of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time are out, and most reviewers are certain this one has avoided the pitfalls of some remakes – no weird visual redesigns here. Our review will come a little later, but for now, let's look at what some are saying about how Nintendo's 1998 Nintendo 64 adventure, often held up as one of the greatest games ever made, has made it to the Switch 2.

The response has been pretty spectacular. The remake is sitting at 96 on Metacritic, with a hefty collection of perfect scores and very few critics finding much to complain about. That's quite an achievement for a game whose original version already has such a place in gaming history.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake launches on 5 November, and Nintendo has done rather more than give Link a fresh coat of paint. Hyrule has been rebuilt with a striking new visual style (while staying tonally true), the soundtrack has been re-orchestrated, and the original's occasionally clunky controls have been updated. Now it offers free jumping, a modern camera, and fully voiced cutscenes, alongside redesigned dungeons and expanded character interactions.

It's a substantial overhaul, even if the adventure itself remains reassuringly familiar to those who grew up on this classic Nintendo game. If you're a fan, take our Ultimate Zelda quiz and test your knowledge.

GamesRadar+ awards the remake five stars, calling it "a remake for the ages". Reviewer Anna Koselke is particularly taken with how Nintendo has brought Hyrule to life without losing the original's charm, praising its "stunning visuals" and newly voiced dialogue.

TechRadar is equally impressed, awarding five stars and describing the remake as "a thoroughly well-thought-out retelling of one of the most loved video games of all time". Reviewer Josephine Watson highlights the redesigned locations, enemies and puzzles, as well as the refreshed soundtrack. There is a little caution, though, with TechRadar suggesting the remake could have been more ambitious and that some of the original's difficulty has been lost along the way.

Game Informer awards the remake 9.25/10, describing it as "a straightforward recreation. But an admittedly very good one." Reviewer Kyle Hilliard likes the visual overhaul and improved camera but feels Nintendo has played it relatively safe with the changes. It looks new, he argues, without necessarily feeling like an entirely new adventure.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Shacknews is considerably more enthusiastic, awarding a perfect 10/10 and calling the remake "a tour de force that redefines what remakes can be". Its review highlights how Nintendo has made familiar mechanics feel fresh, from the reworked combat to the redesigned dungeons. It also singles out Navi, who has been given a much bigger role as a companion rather than simply popping up to shout "Hey! Listen!"

Destructoid also hands out a 10/10, calling it "one of the best examples of how to properly reimagine an enduring masterpiece". The review praises the improved visuals, 60fps performance, and updated controls, though it agrees the remake can feel a little too easy in places.

The Guardian's Keza MacDonald is similarly impressed, scoring the game five stars and describing the remake as "a dream come true". Her review makes an interesting observation about the new art direction, comparing Hyrule to detailed theatre scenery and Link to a handmade doll, with his equipment looking like exaggerated props. It's a more stylised approach than simply chasing photorealism, and one that suits the fairytale quality of the original.

Reviewers are highlighting the lack of Master Quest is a notable omission for returning players wanting a tougher adventure, while the more generous clues and quality-of-life improvements have softened some of the original's challenge.

GamesRadar+ also flags the absence of Master Quest and the single-save-file restriction as drawbacks. Game Informer, meanwhile, feels the remake could have been a little more adventurous in how it reinterprets the original.

These aren't deal-breakers, but they're worth considering if you've spent years exploring Hyrule and know the N64 version inside out.

For anyone who grew up with the original Nintendo 64, this remake is clearly wrapped in an enormous amount of nostalgia. But Ocarina of Time is more than a fond memory for those of us who played it back in 1998. It helped establish the language of 3D adventure games, from lock-on combat to sprawling interconnected dungeons, and its influence still runs through modern game design.

Nintendo has had to do a lot of work to bring the adventure up to date, but it hasn't had to fix what made it special in the first place. The world design, the sense of discovery, and that slightly odd fairy-tale atmosphere are all still there, only now with the visual detail and fluidity the original hardware could never manage.

And Nintendo is very good at this too, with the recent Star Fox remake also proving popular on Switch 2, and the many Metroid and Mario updates and remasters proving a reason to buy Nintendo Online.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time launches on Nintendo Switch 2 on 5 November.

Enzo Maresca's first season as Manchester City boss isn't all going according to plan so far

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Manchester City’s start to the 2026/27 season under Enzo Maresca has received a blow, with reports emerging that Rayan Ait-Nouri’s representatives are actively and publicly seeking a way out of the Etihad Stadium for their client.

The Algerian international, widely regarded as one of Europe’s more dynamic attacking full-backs, has found himself out of the side under the Italian. Frustration has seemingly now reached a point of no return.

According to L'Equipe, which was subsequently relayed by Get French Football News, Ait-Nouri’s camp has lost patience with Maresca’s handling of the 25-year-old.

The player's entourage delivered a blunt assessment of his current predicament, declaring: "He can’t remain in a situation where his talent is wasted."

That admission provides a window into the growing disconnect between Maresca and certain members his squad. Since taking the reins at the Etihad, the former Chelsea boss has adhered to a tactical style that has bypassed Ait-Nouri’s natural overlapping instincts.

Ait-Nouri’s sidelining could be framed as particularly confusing given Man City’s recent struggles to break down low-block defences - a scenario where the Algerian’s ball-carrying ability and final-third delivery usually prove effective.

Instead, he has been restricted to cameo appearances and domestic cup starts, a downgrade in status that has not gone unnoticed by those representing him.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

The January transfer window now looks a possible escape route for Ait-Nouri, following his camp's public agitating. With his entourage pushing for a resolution, there will no doubt be a host of Champions League contenders closely monitoring the situation, aware that a player of his calibre rarely becomes available mid-season.

For Maresca, the immediate challenge is finding a way to quell the mounting concerns within his ranks and restore order to the squad.

If players like Ait-Nouri feel their prime years are being squandered, and are emboldened enough to speak out about it, there is every chance more marginalised members of Maresca's squad will follow suit.

The 2027 Toyota Sequoia Is Doubling Down on Off-Roading

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Upgraded exterior features and the new Trailhunter package make Toyota’s full-size SUV more capable than ever.

The Toyota Sequoia has been around for 25 years, so it’s no surprise to hear the full-size SUV is getting a serious makeover for the 2027 model year. And upgraded exterior looks and features, big-time towing proficiency, and more off-road capability—complete with a new are all on the menu.

Fresh exterior styling is the most obvious change to the 2027 Sequoia. The new front-end geometry is wider, while the lower bumper integrates new rectangular fog lights; certain Sequoia trim levels also add a new hexagonal grille design. Speaking of, Toyota will offer six versions of the Sequoia for 2027: SR5, Limited, Platinum, 1794, TRD Pro, and Capstone.

Off-road adventure is a big focus of the 2027 lineup, too. Toyota will offer two off-road-focused models—the jumpable TRD Pro trim and a Trailhunter package for the SR5 trim, aimed at overlanding—at two different price points. TRD Pro Sequoia models feature Sequoia-specific FOX QS3 adjustable shocks, 18-inch forged BBS wheels, and available ISO-dynamic seats; standard red tow hooks and a striking new Honeycomb signature color also join the fray. Meanwhile, the Trailhunter package includes an Old Man Emu upgraded suspension, an underbody skid plate, front recovery hooks, and Michelin LTX tires on exclusive bronze-colored wheels. A locking rear differential is also part of the package.

Every Sequoia model is powered by Toyota’s 3.4-liter twin-turbo V-6 hybrid powertrain, offering 437 horsepower and 538 lb-ft of torque. Towing capacity comes in at a whopping 9520 pounds. Toyota added towing-specific features to its Toyota Safety Sense 4.0 technology suite; Automated Trailer Detection, Trailer Blind Spot Monitor, Trailer Lane Trace Assist, and Trailer Backup Guide are all standard for 2027.

Inside, the 2027 Toyota Sequoia offers a standard 14-inch touchscreen and a built-in Drive Recorder Dash Cam with playback available directly through the screen. A 12.3-inch digital instrument cluster is now standard, too.

SR5 trim Sequoia models start at $72,095, while Limited Sequoia models are priced at $78,170, and Platinum versions start at $85,595. The TRD Pro Sequoia is priced at $88,120, and the Capstone tops the lineup at $90,095. Toyota is also offering an $86,595 1794-edition Sequoia with Western-inspired design details, including unique saddle-brown interior accents and open-pore wood trim. And that Trailhunter will run you at least $75,595.

First X-BAT fighter jet prototype unveiled as Shield AI preps Seattle area factory for 2027 launch

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

by John Cook on Nov 4, 2026 at 8:26 amNovember 4, 2026 at 8:41 am

Defense technology startup Shield AI has completed its first full-scale prototype of the X-BAT autonomous fighter jet as the company prepares its manufacturing and engineering hub south of Seattle.

Constructed in 12 months at a facility in Frisco, Texas, the prototype is currently en route to the company’s flight-test center at Newton City-County Airport in Kansas.

The milestone keeps Shield AI on track for its major manufacturing push in the Seattle area, one that it says could employ 4,000 workers in the region by the early 2030s.

In late September, the company announced a 420,000-square-foot facility dubbed “X-Forge 3” at the Des Moines Creek Business Park, which is scheduled to open in spring 2027. The company has already posted dozens of Seattle-area job openings across avionics, mechanical design, software engineering and aircraft manufacturing.

The unveiling of the X-BAT prototype keeps the company on schedule for its planned regional buildout in Des Moines, a spokesperson tells GeekWire.

“This milestone represents a year of sustained effort and discipline from a team that committed to an ambitious design and build schedule and delivered,” Armor Harris, senior vice president of aircraft engineering at Shield AI, said in a blog post. “That is no small feat.”

San Diego-based Shield AI expects the first X-BAT aircraft to roll off the line in Des Moines in 2028, with full production ramping up to about 150 aircraft per year by the early 2030s, Harris told GeekWire in an interview in September.

It’s an extremely ambitious timeline, but one the company says is necessary given the changing dynamics of warfare.

“The team is laser focused on keeping pace because we know a capability is only useful if it arrives in time to matter,” Harris said. “We cannot take decades to develop an aircraft when the need is already here. That urgency has shaped how we approach the engineering work, and how we work with partners in the U.S. government.”

Unveiled last year, the X-BAT is an AI-piloted strike fighter that takes off and lands vertically, carries up to 2,000-pound class weapons in internal bays, and is designed for a 1,000-nautical-mile combat radius — which, when it takes flight, would top the estimated 590 nautical mile combat radius of the Lockheed Martin F-35A.

One of its key tactical advantages is the ability to take off and land without concrete runways — hence the tagline for the X-BAT: “Earth is our Runway.”

The X-BAT launches from and returns to a mobile, trailer-mounted Launch and Recovery Vehicle, which allows the AI-piloted jet to deploy from cargo ships, naval destroyers or remote areas where it’s too costly or dangerous to build traditional runways.

“Our adversaries have invested in long range missiles that put our aircraft at risk when they’re on the ground. Aircraft are hard to shoot down in the air, but easy to destroy when they’re on the ground,” Harris notes in the blog post. “X-BAT is the answer to this threat because it forces the adversary to take all those assets that are overhead looking down at our airbases and makes them try to go and find where the X-BATs are hiding.”

Last month, the U.S. Navy awarded Shield AI a $150 million contract modification through the Pentagon’s Defense Exportability Features program, matched dollar-for-dollar by $150 million in private Shield AI funds. Alongside a prior $100 million joint Navy and Defense Innovation Unit award in August, total program backing stands at approximately $400 million.

Co-founded by former Navy SEAL Brandon Tseng, Shield AI raised a $2 billion venture capital round at a $12.7 billion valuation earlier this year.

With the prototype built, Shield AI’s engineering team is focusing on proving out its vertical-takeoff-and-landing mechanics in Kansas ahead of initial flight trials later this year.

Harris detailed a multi-phase testing campaign ahead:

“We’ve built the first aircraft,” Harris wrote. “Now it’s time to go prove what it can do.”

Citi AAdvantage Business Card Loyalty Points Double Dip Explained

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The single best way to earn American AAdvantage Loyalty Points with credit card spending often flies under the radar, so I’d like to talk a little more about that in this post, especially since this tends to cause some confusion. Furthermore, this was recently made an “official” card perk, rather than something that just unofficially applied to most cardmembers.

Link: Double dip your Loyalty Points earning with the Citi® / AAdvantage Business™ World Elite Mastercard®

With the American AAdvantage Loyalty Points program, you can typically earn one Loyalty Point per dollar spent on a co-branded American credit card. However, there’s one card that’s an exception, and by far the best.

The Citi® / AAdvantage Business™ World Elite Mastercard® (learn more) is the co-branded Citi and American Airlines business card. There’s a quirk to the card, though, when it comes to authorized user rewards. On the surface, the authorized user ordinarily earns the Loyalty Points for their spending, and not the primary cardmember, which is an unusual policy for a small business card.

However, through December 31, 2028 (and it’s possible this will be extended again, as it has been in the past), both the primary cardmember and the authorized user earn Loyalty Points for authorized user spending. It basically means you earn two Loyalty Points per dollar spent, just in different accounts. Just to be abundantly clear here:

So the key is for the spending to be on the authorized user’s card, so that this spending unlocks double Loyalty Points (spread across two accounts).

Either way, the Citi AAdvantage Business Card offers several valuable perks, and also has an excellent welcome bonus, making it a good time to apply. So if you are eligible, I’d consider applying even if the double Loyalty Points opportunity doesn’t work out (though hopefully it does).

Personally, the Citi AAdvantage Business Card Loyalty Points double dip is how I qualified for AAdvantage elite status last year. I had a hard time justifying putting spending onto co-branded American cards compared to some other cards out there, when I was only earning one Loyalty Point per dollar.

But if I can qualify for elite status for two people at once, and earn the corresponding Loyalty Point Rewards perks, the math on that spending starts to look much better:

For example, earning 250,000 Loyalty Points per account could potentially earn you a combined total of 12 systemwide upgrades or 170,000 bonus miles. That’s a pretty substantial reward, beyond just the miles you earn for spending and the status!

The Citi AAdvantage Business Card offers an easy opportunity to double dip Loyalty Points earning through authorized user spending. This opportunity is available to all cardmembers, and is currently valid through December 31, 2028 (though has the potential to be extended beyond that). This is a great opportunity, so if you’re pursuing AAdvantage elite status, I’d highly recommend this method.

Have you been taking advantage of this Loyalty Points double dip opportunity?

Top-scoring attacking trios in 2026-27: Barcelona way out in front already

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Bayern Munich had the best front three in Europe last season, but there’s already a different runaway leader this time around.

We’ve taken a closer look at the highest-scoring trios across the Premier League, La Liga, Serie A, Bundesliga and Ligue 1 this season. It doesn’t matter whether the three players were on the pitch at the same time or not, just how many goals each team’s three best scorers have got.

It’s very much the early days of this ranking, so expect plenty of change as the season wears on.

But for now, here are the top 10 attacking trios in Europe’s top five leagues this season.

Barcelona have banished any fears they’d have a void up front after the Pole’s exit this summer, with Raphinha reborn as a centre-forward. And a pretty good one at that, too.

Lamine Yamal is in his typically outrageous form as well, and so Barca are way out in front with their best three-way combination for goals this season.

We imagine five-goal Fermin Lopez might not be part of the trio for much longer, since he isn’t really playing as an attacker anyway. Anthony Gordon, your time will come.

Jose Mourinho’s second Real Madrid reign hasn’t been the most convincing so far, but it’s helped that some of his star attackers are still doing the business.

That’s especially the case with Kylian Mbappe, who’s scored 12 goals in La Liga so far – including a brace in El Clasico.

Jude Bellingham has chipped in with a few as well and summer signing Yan Diomande has netted three times.

Highlights have included a 3-0 win at Fiorentina, beating Cesc Fabregas’ Como and knocking four past Sassuolo and five past Lecce.

Antonio Raimondo leads the way with eight, with midfielder Giacomo Calo adding five and winger Giorgi Kvernadze contributing three. Consider them firmly on our radar.

Raimondo will be out for a few months though with an injury, just as he was starting to flourish. Football can be cruel.

Esteban Lepaul is the star here, with seven goals already after netting 20 last season. No wonder he recently made his international debut for France.

Boulaye Dia and Sebastien Szymanski have chipped in with four each too.

Never mind the off-field distractions, it’s been business as usual on the pitch for City.

Erling Haaland has gone nine from nine so far in the Premier League and Rayan Cherki has already matched his total of four from last season.

No one quite knows what these goals are going towards, but until a recent slip-up or two, City are by and large just beating what’s in front of them for now.

Last year’s most prolific trio are really starting to kick up a gear, scoring five goals between them in Bayern’s 6-1 win over RB Leipzig.

Michael Olise currently leads the way with six, but Luis Diaz was the hat-trick scorer against Leipzig and Kane will hit double figures before your next bedtime.

Roma have been enjoying their highest-scoring 10-game run at the start of a season in Serie A with 28 goals.

Half of those have come from Donyell Malen, Rodrigo Mora and Matias Soule, which has become their best option for a front three while Paulo Dybala has been injured.

It’s Malen doing most of the heavy lifting with eight goals in the league so far.

Hang on a minute. Yep, that’s Niclas Fullkrug, the worst Premier League signing of all time.

He’s a man reborn at Werder Bremen this season with nine goals. Shoutout to Marco Grull as well, who’s assisted four of them (and three for other teammates) as well as scoring twice himself.

JP Morgan? CPR? We’ve seen some frankly awful shouts for what Chelsea’s new front three should go by, but one thing’s for sure – they seem to have clicked straight away.

Morgan Rogers was the missing ingredient, joining Joao Pedro and Cole Palmer in a big-money (OK, huge money) move from Aston Villa in the summer.

He’s hit the ground running with five goals in the league so far, the same amount as striker Pedro.

An attacking trio featuring a midfielder, a 20-year-old and a wing-back. Fair enough.

Milan will be hoping big summer signing Goncalo Ramos can start repaying their faith, but for now, their main scorers have been Adrien Rabiot, Alphadjo Cisse and Diego Moreira.

Bright turns on Ocusell integration so agents enter listings once

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Bright MLS launched a live integration with Ocusell’s network, letting agents enter and edit listings once and syndicate to Bright and other MLSs. Ocusell says it can cut listing creation time up to 75% and prevent over 85% of compliance errors.  AI Summary

Bright MLS has turned on its live integration with Ocusell’s listing network, allowing brokers and agents who work across multiple MLSs in the Mid-Atlantic to enter and edit listings once and syndicate them into Bright and other participating MLSs without double data entry, the companies announced Tuesday.

The operational launch marks the first major product milestone from the joint venture Bright and Ocusell formed in 2025 and introduces OneList, a co-developed listing input platform built on the Ocusell Network that is designed to give brokerages and MLSs a single, connected listing interface nationwide.

“We entered into this joint venture to remove friction from the daily work of our brokers and agents,” said Bright MLS President and CEO Brian Donnellan, who is also a HousingWire Vanguard award winner. “Our role as an MLS is to adapt to how real estate pros operate and to help them succeed. This partnership with Ocusell is a clear example of that.”

The launch comes as the Open Real Estate (ORE) initiative is running a national request for proposal search for a vendor that can deliver “input once, distribute everywhere” listing entry across markets. Ocusell says it is already live across 30 MLSs, and with OneList now operating at Bright, one of the nation’s largest MLSs, the company is positioning its network as a scalable solution for multi-MLS entry.

For brokers, the integration is aimed at reducing staff time and compliance risk tied to re-keying listing data into separate MLS systems. Agents can enter listing specifics, photos and disclosures once; the platform then auto-validates those fields against each local MLS’s rules while maintaining existing access and display policies. According to Ocusell, its infrastructure can cut listing creation time by up to 75% and prevent more than 85% of compliance errors.

On top of cross-market sync, according to the announcement, the forthcoming OneList interface layers in real-time AI-driven compliance and Fair Housing screening, intelligent media management with automated photo tagging and organization, and integrated listing promotion that lets agents launch targeted marketing campaigns from the listing input workflow.

“This is a total game changer for agents, brokers, and home buyers and sellers,” said Steve Mapes, executive in charge of growth strategy at Bright MLS. “When agents aren’t stuck doing double data entry because they belong to multiple MLSs, they have more time to serve their clients and do what they do best. Technology should make things better, faster and more efficient, and we’re excited to get this up and running.”

“Now is the time for MLSs to help shape what comes next for their brokers,” Hayden Rieveschl, the CEO of Ocusell and a 2026 HousingWire Insider, said in a statement. “Bright and Ocusell are demonstrating what’s possible when you listen to brokers and turn their needs into action. As more brokerages operate across markets, MLSs have an opportunity to remove barriers and make doing business easier.”

The move reflects a broader shift in organized real estate as large regional brokerages, teams and franchises increasingly span multiple MLS territories while regulators and plaintiffs’ attorneys scrutinize how listing data is shared and displayed.

For brokers and agents, multi-MLS input has historically meant duplicate work, inconsistent data, higher error rates and delays getting listings in front of buyers across markets. A centralized entry layer like OneList, if widely adopted, could streamline operations for multi-market firms, tighten compliance controls and create more standardized listing data for downstream tools, portals and valuation models.

For MLS organizations, the Bright-Ocusell rollout is a signal that listing input may increasingly be handled through connected, vendor-operated platforms rather than bespoke MLS-only interfaces, potentially reshaping how MLSs think about technology procurement, interoperability and data licensing.

Ocusell said the integration is live now for active Bright MLS brokerages that manage listings across multiple markets, according to the announcement. Bright said rollout timelines and early-access details for the broader OneList platform will be shared in future communications.

cantilevered glass and concrete pavilion by APAP extends 1970s woodland house in nicosia

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

defined by shifting woodland shadows by day, the pavilion transforms at night through a tailored lighting installation that recasts its architectural geometry.

APAP edited by christina vergopoulou November 4, 2026 3 min

Designed by architecture practice APAP, DIAGONAL is a residential extension to a 1970s dwelling situated on a wooded, elevated plot in Nicosia, Cyprus. Departing from the original home’s primary structural motif, defined by two parallel load-bearing walls, the project introduces an unprogrammed pavilion articulated by two horizontal planes that cantilever outward toward the central courtyard, establishing direct visual connections with the surrounding tree canopy.

Conceived by APAP Studio without a rigid functional brief, the open volume adapts to indeterminate domestic uses over time. Glazed perimeters capture the shifting patterns of foliage shadows and diurnal sunlight, turning the concrete and glass envelope into a receptive plane for natural light. After dusk, the volume functions as an illuminated vitrine via a custom four-part diagonal lighting armature suspended within the space. The bespoke fixture deploys three distinct color temperatures: very warm red light cast toward the sides, very cool white light directed up toward the ceiling, and warmer white light projected down onto the floor. This calibrated artificial lighting schema emphasizes the structural diagonal of the enclosure without requiring internal physical partitions.

Giant Impacts Help Rocky Planets Retain Their Atmospheres

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Earth's formation was a messy process. It all happened about 4.5 billion years ago, in our young chaotic Solar System. Rocky planetesimals were whizzing around in the Solar System back then, and Earth formed as more of these objects collided and merged with it.

Some of these collisions were giant impacts that generated immense heat. These were common in the later stages of Earth's formation, after the protoplanetary disk had largely dissipated and atmospheric accretion was complete. Planetary scientists have wondered how young planets like Earth could've held onto their atmospheres near the end of their accretion period. The massive heat from giant impacts should've driven primordial He/H atmospheres away. But new research suggests that simple picture doesn't capture some important details, and that giant impacts could've actually helped young rocky planets retain their atmospheres.

The work is titled "Retention of primordial atmospheres through giant impacts," and it's published in Nature Astronomy. The authors are Darius Modirrousta-Galian and Jun Korenaga. Modirrousta-Galian is a former Yale postdoc who is now at Shanghai Jiao Tong University, and Korenaga is a professor of Earth and planetary sciences in Yaleâs Faculty of Arts and Sciences.

This research focuses on what happens near the end of a rocky planet's accretion process, after the protoplanetary disk has largely dissipated. This is when other planetesimals had also grown large through their own accretion processes, and when they collided with a planet like Earth, they released an enormous amount of energy.

âGiant impacts are a key part of how rocky planets grow,â said co-author Korenaga. âPlanets the size of Earth or larger are expected to form through collisions between smaller planetary bodies. These collisions add mass and release enormous amounts of heat, which can drive atmospheric escape. Their effect on a planetâs atmosphere depends on how those two changes work together.â

Modirrousta-Galian and Korenaga have been working on how planetary interiors and atmospheres evolve together and have published several papers on the topic. Their previous research laid the foundation for this work.

Their work shows that increased heat near a planet's surface isn't the only result of impacts. Some of that heat is driven deep into the planet. The impact can also "increase atmospheric gravitational binding," according to the researchers.

There are two components of a giant impact: heat and mass.

This illustration shows planetesimals in a disk around a young star. Rocky planets grow by accretion of planetesimals, and as time goes on, the planetesimals become more massive. When these massive planetesimals impact young planets, they not only generate heat, they add mass. Image Credit: NASA / JPL-Caltech.

When a planetesimal smashes into a rocky planet, the heat can be divided into two separate components. Some of it is driven more deeply into the planet where it won't drive away the atmosphere. Some of it will be mantle heat that does contribute to driving the primordial atmosphere away. But the mass gained by the accretion of the planetesimal isn't divided. All of that mass gives the planet more power to hold onto its atmosphere.

"Whereas the entire accreted mass contributes to the increase in gravitational binding energy, only part of the impact energy raises mantle temperature," the authors explain. "Mantle melting consumes energy as latent heat, while the impactorâs metallic core transfers little energy to the mantle and remains superheated at depth."

"Giant impacts therefore increase gravitational binding energy more than mantle thermal energy, shifting planets towards atmospheric retention," the researchers explain.

This understanding can help explain the exoplanet population.

Astronomers used to think that our Solar System was an archetype of how other solar systems would look. There would be rocky planets in the inner Solar System, gas giants further out, and ice giants beyond them. But exoplanet discoveries have cast doubt on that. The most common types of detected exoplanets aren't even represented in our Solar System: super-Earths and sub-Neptunes.

Astronomers think that super-Earths and sub-Neptunes could represent two types of planets that started out as rocky cores but ended up with much different atmospheres. Super-Earths have lost their atmospheres, while sub-Neptunes have retained more of their primordial atmospheres. A sub-Neptune only needs to retain a few percent of its H/He atmosphere to have an extended puffy atmosphere that can increase its radius by a large amount.

These are artist's impressions of the super-Earth Kepler-452b (right) and the sub-Neptune Kepler-22b (left). They're very different from each other, and both types of exoplanet are abundant in exoplanet discoveries. Giant impacts could explain their abundance. Image Credit: NASA.

âWhy nature produces both types in such abundance is a fundamental puzzle in exoplanetary science,â said Modirrousta-Galian. âWe set out to find an explanation.â

This research shows that sub-Neptunes could have grown larger through more massive impacts, and those impacts not only added mass but allowed them to retain larger atmospheres. Super-Earth's weren't subjected to those same massive impacts, and so did not gain enough mass to retain their atmospheres the same way that sub-Neptunes did. They lost theirs due to stellar radiation.

This schematic shows different outcomes for rocky planets. In one scenario, massive impacts onto a rocky create a more massive planet that can retain more of its primordial atmosphere: a sub-Neptune. In the other scenario, there are no massive impacts, no increase in rocky mass, and the planet retains less of its atmosphere. Image Credit: Darius Modirrousta-Galian

"Larger, atmosphere-bearing planets emerge preferentially through collisional growth," the researchers explain. "By contrast, smaller planets without such impacts tend to lose their primordial atmospheres under sustained stellar irradiation. At the population level, these results suggest that sub-Neptunes have, on average, more massive rocky interiors than super-Earths."

These results appear to overturn the long-held idea that giant impacts spelled doom for planetary atmospheres, and in turn, for the prospects of habitability. Primordial H/He atmospheres themselves didnt create habitability. But some research shows that they're essential for the development of a world that is eventually habitable, through interactions with a young rocky planet's magma ocean. These interactions may have been a significant source of Earth's water and could be on other rocky worlds, too.

âThis overturns the decades-old canonical view that giant impacts almost always lead to complete atmospheric loss, demonstrating instead that these collisions can play a crucial role in the formation of sub-Neptunes,â Korenaga said.

Evan Gough is a science-loving guy with no formal education who loves Earth, forests, hiking, and heavy music. He's guided by Carl Sagan's quote: "Understanding is a kind of ecstasy."

“We Used FFS and Were Targeting Big Bass.” Angler Breaks 76-Year-Old Smallmouth Bass Record

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 4, 2026 4:14 PM EST

The hottest fishing news, tips, and tactics

On the morning of October 30 Austin Schiefer and his fishing buddy Ted Johnson launched their bass boat into Green Bay, a huge Wisconsin portion of Lake Michigan. They started about 7:30 working a reef area in 18 feet of water about two miles from shore. Before long, they’d go on to break a 76-year-old fishing record.

“It was a spot we’d fished previously and caught nice smallmouths on,” Schiefer tells Outdoor Life. “We used forward-facing sonar and were targeting big bass.”

The morning started windy, ideal conditions for clear water bass on Green Bay. The duo was catching bass, but about mid-day the wind died and the water got calm.

“That’s not the best conditions for Green Bay,” says Schiefer, who works for UPS when he’s not producing his own line of soft plastic lures (Schief’s Custom Baits). “There were plenty of bass on the reef, but they were finicky and wouldn’t take the lures I was casting.”

Johnson, however, was catching bass using a different type of soft plastic. He suggested to Schiefer that he try one of the lures he was doing well with, and to go lighter with his tackle.

“The bite was light and deep, so I changed to the setup that Ted suggested,” says Schiefer, from Menasha, Wisconsin.

Schiefer changed over to a Deps Sakamata Shad Soft Jerkbait, which is a 5-inch lure, and the productive hue was “champagne-pepper.”

He threaded the lure onto a 3/32nd-ounce jighead and made a long cast with the lightest 7-foot spinning rod he owns. His reel was spooled with 8-pound test Power Pro braided line with a 10-pound test fluorocarbon leader.

“I wanted to make a long cast and sort of swim the lure back as it sank, a pendulum like retrieve,” says Schiefer. “We could see fish on bottom with sonar, but they’d rise up to take lures. Except they were refusing my lures just before they’d hit – until I cast that Deps Jerkbait.”

It was about 2 p.m. and as Schiefer’s lure sank he felt a hit. But the fish didn’t fight like a smallmouth. The battle wasn’t fast and furious and there were no jumps. Just a couple minutes of it taking drag, and rugged deep pulling and rolling – so much so that Schiefer thought perhaps he’d hooked a freshwater drum.

“But as I worked it to the surface I got a look at it in that clear water about 10 feet down, and I saw that it was a huge smallmouth,” he says. “I knew it was a monster, and when I got it to the boat Ted netted it.

“I got lucky, because the hook was bent when I took the lure out of its mouth.”

Schiefer had caught big smallmouths previously, but nothing over 7 pounds, so he knew his catch was important. They put the lunker bass in their boat livewell and tried to contact Wisconsin’s Department of Natural Resources. But it was Friday afternoon before Halloween, and no one from the DNR was available.

The anglers quit fishing and trailered their boat to a grocery store where the bass was weighed at 9 pounds, 11ounces on certified scales. It measured 23.75 inches long. The smallmouth easily topped the 1950 Wisconsin record smallie weighing 9 pounds, 1 ounce. That fish was caught by Leon Stefonek in Oneida County’s Indian Lake.

To qualify as a state record bass, the DNR required the fish to be weighed and examined by state biologists. That didn’t happen until the next day, when DNR fisheries staff weighed and inspected the fish.

They discovered the bass was a male, not a female – which usually are the larger of the two fish genders. Additionally, Schiefer’s 9.70-pound smallmouth had nothing in its stomach, again unusual as most bass in autumn are feeding heavily for the coming winter.

Schiefer will have a skin mount made of his smallmouth bass record, which he says has been officially recognized by DNR as Wisconsin’s heaviest bronzeback of all time. He’s also considering having replica mounts made of the fish.

Read Next: 7 Forward Facing Sonar Tips: Settings, Mounting, and Techniques Explained

“I think a replica mount of the bass would be nice for the National Freshwater Fishing Hall of Fame in their Hayward museum, and maybe one for Bass Pro Shops, too,” he says.

“My only regret in all of this is I didn’t release the fish alive. I couldn’t with the DNR requirements for weighing and inspecting the fish for the record… I’ve had some sleepless nights because of that. I did everything in my power to keep from killing that bass.”

Bob McNally has been an outdoor writer since shortly after the earth’s crust cooled. He has written 12 outdoor books, more than 5,000 outdoor magazine stories (including many for Outdoor Life) and more newspaper outdoor columns and features than there are hairs on a grizzly bear.

China Eastern’s Ex-Chairman Gets 14 Years For $10 Million In Bribes—He Didn’t Follow Xi Jinping’s Instructions

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

China Eastern’s former chairman has been sentenced to 14 years in prison in a bribery case involving more than $10 million. The court says Liu Shaoyong sold favors over a period stretching from 2002 through 2023, using positions at both China Eastern and China Southern. He’d left China Eastern in 2022 with a glowing sendoff from the board. Three years later, the Communist Party announced it was investigating him. Corruption can be real, many state bureaucrats are at least a little corrupt Prosecuting often serves a political purpose. A conviction tells us what (who) the government decided to punish. So what’s always interesting in these cases is why this one, and why now. The Shenyang Intermediate People’s Court handed down its verdict on November 3. It found that Liu took more than 67.5 million yuan in bribes (US$10.1 million) in exchange for help with business operations, project contracts, and airline route management. He was also fined 4 million yuan (US$600,000) and disgorgement of his criminal proceeds. Liu admitted guilt at his July 24 trial. The court says it gave him a lighter sentence because he confessed and disclosed bribery facts investigators hadn’t yet uncovered. The party’s earlier disciplinary findings accused him of taking money for hiring favors, improperly holding shares in unlisted companies, helping relatives’ businesses including private equity funds, and accepting payments connected with aircraft leasing and flight management. There were also allegations about gifts, banquets, golf outings, resisting the investigation, and superstitious activities. A former pilot and deputy head of the country’s aviation regulator, Liu went on to lead China Southern before returning to China Eastern in 2008. He was credited with helping turn around both airlines. At China Eastern, his tenure included the Shanghai Airlines merger, entry into SkyTeam, and bringing private investors into parts of the business. The Party Was Already Looking At China Eastern I’ve written about this before with the detention of HNA’s leaders and the government’s takeover of Anbang Insurance. With Liu, there’s a more concrete place to start than guessing which faction he offended. A central party inspection team examined China Eastern from October through December 2023. A February 2025 report covered corruption leads and tightening controls over procurement. Liu was expelled from the party in January 2026. The report puts following Xi Jinping’s instructions, supporting Chinese aircraft, operating at Beijing Daxing, and strengthening party supervision alongside its discussion of corruption. So this was as much about carrying out the party’s priorities as about how he conducted business. The inspection records don’t tell us what finally triggered Liu’s prosecution. A 2020 report flagged shortcomings in integrating party leadership into companies with outside shareholders. It seems like Liu’s efforts at privatization may have provided insufficient Party control. Developing Beijing Daxing even when the airline had commercial reasons to prefer Capital airport. Reporting in 2019 described China Eastern resisting a wholesale move, with concerns about losing business travelers on routes like Beijing–Shanghai. Liu nevertheless publicly outlined plans for around 200 aircraft at Daxing. He’d left China Eastern in 2022 with a glowing sendoff from the board. Three years later, the Communist Party announced it was investigating him.

Corruption can be real, many state bureaucrats are at least a little corrupt Prosecuting often serves a political purpose. A conviction tells us what (who) the government decided to punish. So what’s always interesting in these cases is why this one, and why now. The Shenyang Intermediate People’s Court handed down its verdict on November 3. It found that Liu took more than 67.5 million yuan in bribes (US$10.1 million) in exchange for help with business operations, project contracts, and airline route management. He was also fined 4 million yuan (US$600,000) and disgorgement of his criminal proceeds. Liu admitted guilt at his July 24 trial. The court says it gave him a lighter sentence because he confessed and disclosed bribery facts investigators hadn’t yet uncovered. The party’s earlier disciplinary findings accused him of taking money for hiring favors, improperly holding shares in unlisted companies, helping relatives’ businesses including private equity funds, and accepting payments connected with aircraft leasing and flight management. There were also allegations about gifts, banquets, golf outings, resisting the investigation, and superstitious activities. He was credited with helping turn around both airlines. The Shenyang Intermediate People’s Court handed down its verdict on November 3. It found that Liu took more than 67.5 million yuan in bribes (US$10.1 million) in exchange for help with business operations, project contracts, and airline route management. He was also fined 4 million yuan (US$600,000) and disgorgement of his criminal proceeds.

Liu admitted guilt at his July 24 trial. The court says it gave him a lighter sentence because he confessed and disclosed bribery facts investigators hadn’t yet uncovered.

The party’s earlier disciplinary findings accused him of taking money for hiring favors, improperly holding shares in unlisted companies, helping relatives’ businesses including private equity funds, and accepting payments connected with aircraft leasing and flight management. There were also allegations about gifts, banquets, golf outings, resisting the investigation, and superstitious activities.

A former pilot and deputy head of the country’s aviation regulator, Liu went on to lead China Southern before returning to China Eastern in 2008. He was credited with helping turn around both airlines. At China Eastern, his tenure included the Shanghai Airlines merger, entry into SkyTeam, and bringing private investors into parts of the business.

I’ve written about this before with the detention of HNA’s leaders and the government’s takeover of Anbang Insurance. With Liu, there’s a more concrete place to start than guessing which faction he offended.

A central party inspection team examined China Eastern from October through December 2023. A February 2025 report covered corruption leads and tightening controls over procurement. Liu was expelled from the party in January 2026.

The report puts following Xi Jinping’s instructions, supporting Chinese aircraft, operating at Beijing Daxing, and strengthening party supervision alongside its discussion of corruption. So this was as much about carrying out the party’s priorities as about how he conducted business.

The inspection records don’t tell us what finally triggered Liu’s prosecution. They do show specific problems during his tenure, and where running the airline could run into the party’s priorities:

Bringing in private investors while maintaining party control. Liu’s 2017 restructuring of Eastern Air Logistics left China Eastern’s group with 45% ownership, outside investors with 45%, and employees with 10%. A 2020 report flagged shortcomings in integrating party leadership into companies with outside shareholders. It seems like Liu’s efforts at privatization may have provided insufficient Party control.

Developing Beijing Daxing even when the airline had commercial reasons to prefer Capital airport. Reporting in 2019 described China Eastern resisting a wholesale move, with concerns about losing business travelers on routes like Beijing–Shanghai. Liu nevertheless publicly outlined plans for around 200 aircraft at Daxing. The government called for more capacity, a better route network, and faster construction of facilities supporting maintenance. By late 2024, party discipline officials were monitoring aircraft deployment and passenger numbers there. Liu resisted building up the Party’s showcase airport.

Making Chinese aircraft succeed commercially. Liu was there when China Eastern signed its first firm order for five C919s in March 2021. The airline had also established a subsidiary to operate Chinese regional jets. The C919 only received its type certificate in September 2022, after he left. So Liu didn’t refuse Chinese planes. But the government called for broader route coverage, stronger marketing, and better operating support. They bought the planes but didn’t make the aircraft program work (and they can’t really blame the aircraft, so blame the airlines).

Following Xi’s safety instructions. The report published in March 2020 described units giving insufficient attention to important safety and operating issues, failing to escalate major safety matters, and failing to act effectively on identified problems. It called for stronger safety oversight, clearer accountability, and changes involving flight operations, maintenance, and operational control.

China Eastern had a crash after the report where the party said Liu’s airline needed to improve. China Eastern flight 5735 crashed in March 2022 and killed 132 people. Liu left the group’s top job four months later. In June 2025, a scholar suggested the crash helped trigger scrutiny of him.

Prosecuting Liu enforces discipline over top leadership running Chinese state airlines. I don’t know which patron, rival, or specific transaction made Liu vulnerable.

China Eastern’s August 2022 resignation filing said Liu had “faithfully and diligently performed his duties” and thanked him for his “outstanding contributions.” The court now says his bribery stretched through 2023—beyond that farewell.

Huizenga Loses House Seat Despite $1 Million From Crypto PAC Fairshake

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Rep. Bill Huizenga, vice chair of the House Financial Services Committee, lost despite $1 million from Fairshake.

Rep. Bill Huizenga, the No. 2 Republican on the House Financial Services Committee, lost his reelection bid to Democrat Sean McCann, the Associated Press called Wednesday afternoon, despite about $1 million in support from the crypto super PAC Fairshake.

With more than 97% of votes counted earlier today, McCann led Huizenga 50.1% to 48.3% in Michigan’s 4th Congressional District, a gap of about 7,000 votes out of nearly 300,000 cast. The race had been rated “lean Republican” heading into Tuesday.

Huizenga, an eight-term congressman and the committee’s vice chair, is the highest-ranking Republican on the panel to lose. He is not the only crypto ally on it to go down.

Rep. Bryan Steil of Wisconsin, who chairs the Subcommittee on Digital Assets, Financial Technology and Artificial Intelligence, lost to Democrat Mitchell Berman 52% to 48%. The AP called that race at 11:48 p.m. Tuesday.

Rep. Ann Wagner of Missouri, who chairs the capital markets subcommittee, lost to Democrat Fred Wellman. Rep. Zach Nunn of Iowa lost to state Sen. Sarah Trone Garriott, and Rep. Mike Lawler of New York conceded to Democrat Cait Conley before the AP called his race.

Fairshake committed at least $1 million in October to each of six House incumbents, among them Huizenga, Steil and committee Chairman French Hill of Arkansas. The super PAC reported spending $1,019,166 to support Huizenga in the general election, according to Federal Election Commission filings.

All 32 House incumbents the Fairshake network backed this fall voted for the CLARITY Act when it passed the House 294-134 in July 2025, CNBC reported. The network entered September with about $120.4 million in cash.

The crypto money was a small slice of the Huizenga race. Outside groups reported more than $25 million in spending in the district, FEC filings show, with House Majority PAC alone putting about $6 million against the Republican.

Two of Fairshake’s three Republican $1 million picks on the committee have now lost in midterms. Hill’s own race had not been called by the AP as of Wednesday afternoon. He led Democrat Chris Jones by about 4,400 votes, or 50.9% to 49.1%, with 99% of the expected vote counted.

Democrats won control of the House on Tuesday, which hands the committee gavel to their side regardless of Hill’s result. Rep. Maxine Waters of California, the panel’s top Democrat and its chair from 2019 to 2023, is in line to return to the post. Nineteen Financial Services Democrats backed her in October against a possible challenge from Rep. Sean Casten of Illinois, Politico reported.

Steil’s loss also leaves the digital assets subcommittee without its chair. Huizenga sat on that panel too. Journalist Eleanor Terrett noted that the subcommittee’s name, scope and membership are set each Congress, so Democrats could rename, re-scope or eliminate it.

Huizenga played a role in moving the stablecoin bill President Donald Trump signed in 2025, and he led the committee’s investigations into former SEC Chair Gary Gensler during the Biden administration.

The industry’s main legislative goal is still the CLARITY Act, which failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15. House Democrats are already weighing their own market structure bill with a bigger role for the SEC.

Crypto groups spent heavily to avoid this outcome, with about $176 million committed to more than 100 races this cycle. Losing Huizenga and Steil removes two of the Republicans who shaped crypto legislation on the committee, and both seats flip to Democrats.

Bitcoin itself rallied after the Democratic sweep, even as the industry’s allies in Congress lost ground.

Watch live, catch up on episodes, and get exclusive content on the go.

Bitcoin Magazine is published by BTC Inc., a subsidiary of Nakamoto Inc. (NASDAQ: NAKA).
