The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel fans ponder split timelines, alternative histories, and more as story speculation runs wild following latest teaser

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Following the release of yesterday's brief new The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel teaser trailer, story speculation is running rampant across communities dedicated to the fantasy series, with fans theorising everything from alternative histories to split timelines.

Yesterday's teaser - which arrived as part of Bethesda's 40th anniversary celebrations and comes amid devastating cuts across Xbox's first-party studios - was minimal. It opened with a shot of desert sand, before revealing the subtitle 'Sentinel' and ending with a quote from an unknown character. It's not much to go on, but is proving just enough to encourage discussion around what may be in store for Bethesda's next RPG.

And a good place to start is that quote. "The scroll offered only a shimmer, a veil thinly lifted to my eyes," it reads. "A future lost. A victory undone. Sands of time scattered by fate." All this is attributed to Yazar, Moth Priest of High Queen Iradne of Hammerfell, , but some fans think the accompanying date - 4E 206 - might be just as significant.

"[Sentinel] likely takes place five or more years after the start of Skyrim," hypothesised Reddit user 04nc1n9. "Hammerfell is a monarchy, it hasn't changed to any other political system after their imperial secession. The monarchy of Hammerfell has a Moth Priest on hand, even though the Moth Priests are generally an Imperial thing. Hammerfell's monarch has access to an Elder Scroll after the Elder Scrolls scattered."

This unexpected link between Moth Priests and Hammerfell's High Queen has created speculation of its own. One theory, combined with the proximity of Sentinel's story to that of Skyrim, has the Thalmor play a key role. "[It's] likely the Thalmor being the antagonists with so little time having passed," wrote user Jonesketi, "and with justifiable knowledge of how to cause great harm with the Elder Scrolls, [this] would've prompted [Hammerfell] to begin their own preparations. [The Thalmor are] also such a fitting antagonist since we didn't get to kick them down in Skyrim, which would offer catharsis if it came in Sentinel."

As for the quote itself, its vague wording has inspired some particularly out-there speculation. Some fans are looking back to previously vanquished forces in the series, in light of the phrase "a victory undone", and are theorising it might refer to a Dragon Break - an in-universe timeline split in which multiple events can all occur and simultaneously be true once the timelines reconverge. In previous games, Dragon Breaks have served as a handy explanation for the multiple endings.

Talk of "undone" events set user Wafelze's mind to the Numidium, a giant mechanical golem whose activation in The Elder Scrolls 2: Daggerfall caused its own Dragon Break. "With how important time is to Skyrim’s story," they speculated, "it wouldn’t surprise me if this story is about time being entirely broken."

"See, I'm thinking it has to do with the creation of Nirn and Lorkhan," wrote Potential Film 7140. "The Altmer hate Lorkhan and men see the creation of the world as a victory. The elves did not ... maybe they'll try to undo what created the divines so they can go back to their original ancestry. Wildly out-there take but that's what my brain tells me."

What's more, the theories around split timelines and the Thalmor have converged with thoughts of a possible 'Man in the High Castle'-style alternate history setting for Sentinel, unfolding in a version of the world where the Thalmor have power. "Maybe the Empire originally held together after Oblivion, and the Thalmor used some cheater Elder Scroll magic to rewrite history so that they dunked on the Empire," suggested Original League 6094. "Or if I recall, Hammerfell was doing a great job beating the Thalmor as of Skyrim. Maybe the version of events got overwritten and now Hammerfell is under Thalmor subjugation, and the Elder Scroll is giving her glimpses of the timeline where Hammerfell won."

Will these theories prove to be true? With the launch of Sentinel still reportedly years away, it could be some time before we find out. But if nothing else, this feverish story speculation suggests there's a major hunger among fans for information - any information - about Sentinel. And that's no real surprise. After all, yesterday's teaser was only the second official look at a game whose reveal has been dragging on for over eight years now.

XRP Price Could Hit $10,000? From 21Shares’ Forecast to Influencers’ Moonshots

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

XRP price predictions are stretching from Wall Street’s $2.69 bull case to Jake Claver’s extraordinary $10,000 target, as institutional adoption and crypto’s biggest moonshot forecasts collide.

Asset manager 21Shares sees XRP reaching $2.69 in its optimistic 2026 scenario, while veteran trader Peter Brandt has outlined a longer-term $5.40 target. Standard Chartered’s longer-range forecast reaches $28 by 2030, but XRP’s most aggressive supporters are aiming much higher.

Claver continues to stand by his $10,000 XRP price prediction. The forecast would put XRP’s valuation above the combined value of today’s largest financial markets, turning a bullish debate into one of crypto’s most extreme price calls.

21Shares’ January outlook gave XRP a $2.45 base case for 2026, alongside a $2.69 bullish scenario and a $1.60 bearish outcome. The asset manager tied its bullish case to institutional demand, spot XRP ETF inflows, tokenized real-world assets and growing adoption of Ripple’s financial infrastructure.

Peter Brandt’s $5.40 target comes from a longer-term chart pattern that he believes could eventually push XRP beyond its previous record. Standard Chartered has taken the institutional forecast further, projecting XRP at $28 by 2030 as tokenization and stablecoin adoption expand.

EGRAG Crypto has also outlined a $50 scenario based on historical monthly price patterns. This target would give XRP a market capitalization of roughly $3.15 trillion, assuming around 63 billion tokens circulate.

The forecasts become dramatically more ambitious with Claver. The XRP advocate believes the token could first reach $50 to $100 as banks adopt it as a financial asset. He then expects institutional demand and what he calls a systemic repricing to push XRP toward $10,000.

Claver has previously made a $100 prediction for the end of 2025, a target XRP did not reach. His latest forecast nevertheless keeps the five-figure scenario in view.

XRP’s institutional narrative also highlights a question Bitcoin maximalists have debated for years: can Bitcoin become more than a store of value without sacrificing its core security model? Bitcoin Hyper is positioning itself around this opportunity, building a Bitcoin Layer 2 designed to bring faster transactions and smart-contract functionality to the world’s largest cryptocurrency.

The project combines Bitcoin settlement with Solana Virtual Machine execution, aiming to support applications that Bitcoin’s base layer was not designed to handle directly.

Did somebody order a little extra Bitcoin utility? 👀⚡️https://t.co/VNG0P4FWNQ pic.twitter.com/bQAMiOcdUD

Its HYPER token is currently offered through a presale, with a listing price at just $0.0137 and staking rewards offered at 35% APY. The presale has attracted more than $33 million, showing substantial early investor interest.

That combination of a low entry price, staking incentives, and a Bitcoin-native narrative has helped draw attention from crypto investors looking for infrastructure plays beyond established large-cap assets.

The institutional angle is straightforward: if financial firms increasingly use blockchain networks for settlement, tokenization, and programmable finance, Bitcoin’s ecosystem needs infrastructure capable of supporting those activities.

XRP has spent years building its case around payments and institutional liquidity. Bitcoin Hyper is betting that Bitcoin can attract similar infrastructure demand without asking Bitcoin holders to abandon the network they already trust.

Claver’s forecast remains the most aggressive target in the current XRP prediction lineup. His thesis depends on XRP becoming a major liquidity asset for banks and financial institutions, with demand eventually extending into large-scale derivatives settlement.

No I’ve said $10,000 in late 26 into the first half of 27 also

At $10,000 per token, XRP’s market value would reach approximately $630 trillion based on a circulating supply of 63 billion tokens. That is far beyond the valuation implied by 21Shares’ $2.69 bull case, which puts XRP near $170 billion using the same supply assumption.

The distance between those targets captures the divide in the XRP market. Institutional forecasts focus on ETF demand, adoption, and measurable financial use, while Claver’s moonshot depends on XRP becoming a central component of global financial infrastructure.

Circle's USDC Dominates Stablecoin Market With 79% Share of Transaction Volume

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Circle revealed that USDC accounted for 79% of the transaction volume of all stablecoins in the third quarter of 2026.

According to CEO Jeremy Allaire, the CEO of Circle explained that USDC's market share of the transaction volume of all stablecoins increased significantly from 66% in the same period a year ago. The overall USDC on-chain transaction volume was $13.9 trillion, an increase of 49% year-on-year.

This shows the rapid adoption of USDC in payments, trading, and blockchain-based financial applications despite stiff competition in the stablecoin space.

Circle stated that the issuance and redemption volumes of USDC in the third quarter reached a new high. In total, $208 billion of USDC was issued and redeemed in Q3. This translates to an average daily amount of $2.3 billion. For the third quarter, Circle said that the average USDC in circulation was $73.3 billion, which was up 18% year-on-year. This information was part of Circle's Q3 highlights.

Apart from USDC itself, Circle emphasized the growth in other aspects of its infrastructure. Circle indicated that Arc Mainnet went live in the third quarter. Over 100 institutions and applications are already building on Arc. As of Oct. 30, 2026, assets on Arc totaled $1.5 billion, as stated by Circle.

Circle Payment Network, or CPN, was another part of Circle's infrastructure that witnessed rapid growth. The company indicated that CPN ended the third quarter with an annualized payment volume of $41.3 billion. This was 15.2 times higher than in the same period last year, based on activity over the last 30 days. Allaire explained that CPN's payment volume is now more than $58 billion.

Circle also revealed that USDC has now become the leading settlement asset in agentic/automated internet payments. Allaire disclosed that over 99% of agent payments on x402 are settled in USDC.

In the third quarter, Circle's total revenue and reserve income were $865 million, up 17% year-on-year. This figure included $182 million from ARC token sales, according to an image showing Circle's Q3 highlights.

According to Allaire, the impressive performance in the third quarter is a strong signal that Circle is ready for the next phase of stablecoin adoption. He said the GENIUS Act, which is expected to take effect in January, will bring regulated digital dollars into the mainstream. Also, Circle has now activated all the layers of its platform.

The impressive third-quarter data indicates that Circle has not only further solidified USDC's dominance in terms of transaction activity but is also expanding its ecosystem across payments, on-chain finance, and institutional adoption.

French Bank Credit Mutuel Arkea Confirms Cyberattack as ZachXBT Puts Losses Above $133 Million

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

French bank Credit Mutuel Arkea said Wednesday that a cyberattack allowed hackers to carry out fraudulent transfers from the bank’s own account, a theft that on-chain investigator ZachXBT has put at more than $133 million and that the bank has not confirmed.

The Brittany-based lender said in a statement that the incident was under control, that operations were resuming and that customer assets were not affected. It suspended some transactions as a precaution.

“This incident has had no impact on (customer) assets and has only caused a delay in the execution of certain transfers,” the bank said on its website, in a translation published by Reuters.

In a follow-up question-and-answer note, Credit Mutuel Arkea said the attackers went through the platform of one of its service providers and reached the bank directly. They used the bank’s own account to send transactions to other banks, which then potentially allowed them to convert the money into cryptocurrency through specialized platforms, the bank said.

No customer accounts at its federations or subsidiaries were used, the bank said. It denied French press reports that the attackers got in through Fortuneo, its online banking arm, and said the Credit Mutuel Arkea hack was “a sophisticated attack from the outside,” not the result of human error.

The bank has not given a final figure. Asked whether 118 million euros, or $133 million, had been stolen, it said it does not confirm the number and can only “validate the order of magnitude.”

Part of the funds involved has been frozen and other steps are underway to recover the rest, the bank said. It added that the amounts at stake are “absolutely not” large enough to threaten its financial health or soundness.

AFP reported that the bank put the attack at about 100 million euros in a provisional estimate. Earlier Wednesday, Reuters reported that the bank said its losses were lower than $133 million. “It is still too early to give a final figure,” a spokesperson told the wire service.

ZachXBT first flagged the incident on Monday without naming the victim. He wrote on his Telegram channel that an EU entity was suspected of losing more than $106 million from multiple centralized exchange accounts on Nov. 1, with the funds withdrawn as USDC and USDT. He said the funds were then swapped for ether and moved across several chains.

In an update, he said he had found another $27 million or more in exchange withdrawals, bringing the total to more than $133 million. A later post named Credit Mutuel Arkea as the alleged victim. He wrote that the attack “occurred for multiple days” and that he suspects the amount stolen is larger once over-the-counter activity is counted.

The bank has not confirmed his figure or his timeline. It has also not said when the attack began, who was behind it or whether any data was taken. Credit Mutuel Arkea is working with judicial and police authorities to determine the origin of the attack and how it was carried out. It said it informed its supervisors once the attack was detected.

The bank also warned customers that phishing attempts are possible after the Credit Mutuel Arkea hack.

Credit Mutuel Arkea, which groups the Credit Mutuel federations of Brittany and the Southwest, is part of the wider Credit Mutuel group. It has 5.8 million clients and 211.5 billion euros, or about $238 billion, in assets, Reuters reported. It posted net profit of 230 million euros in the first half of the year, AFP reported.

The Credit Mutuel Arkea hack lands less than a week after the record $6.34 billion Binance hack, which blockchain analysis firms have linked to North Korea. ZachXBT said in his first post that he was highly confident this incident was not the work of North Korea’s Lazarus Group. Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin also froze deposits and withdrawals this week after a security incident.

How much of the money the bank can recover, and what the final loss from the Credit Mutuel Arkea hack will be, remains open.

What housing market headlines can’t tell your clients

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

National housing demand is showing signs of stabilizing, but local market conditions tell a more nuanced story. HousingWire Data shows diverging trends between Houston and its broader metro, rising inventory and pricing pressure in Phoenix, and changing inventory patterns in Tampa. Interactive HousingWire Intelligence charts help housing professionals identify emerging shifts and translate market data into actionable guidance for buyers and sellers.  AI Summary

Mortgage rates are moving closer to 7%, and housing demand is showing signs of stabilizing. But what does that mean for a homeowner preparing to list, a buyer wondering whether to wait or an agent trying to set realistic expectations?

In his latest Housing Market Tracker, HousingWire Lead Analyst Logan Mohtashami explains why a path to sub-7% mortgage rates is back on the table. While weekly pending home sales have recently stabilized, that doesn’t yet signal a sustained recovery.

For housing professionals, the opportunity is to translate those national trends into what clients are experiencing in their own markets.

HousingWire Data from Houston, Phoenix and Tampa reveals three things worth watching:

A client sees that housing demand is stabilizing and asks whether it’s finally a good time to sell.

Houston demonstrates why the answer requires more local context.

Across the Houston metro, the ratio of new pending sales to new listings improved during October, but not because buyer activity increased. New listings declined faster than pending sales, making the ratio look stronger even as fewer homes went under contract.

Within Houston city limits, the opposite happened. Pending sales declined faster than new listings, pushing the ratio lower.

Even inventory told a different story. Active inventory was higher than a year ago across the metro but lower within the city.

Neither market showed growing pending activity, yet the conditions affecting buyers and sellers were different.

For a homeowner, that distinction matters more than whether a regional headline describes the market as improving.

The takeaway: Use broader housing trends to start the conversation, then show clients what’s happening among the homes they’re actually competing with. A seller needs to understand current competition and buyer activity, not simply whether the overall market appears stronger.

A homeowner considering whether to wait for lower mortgage rates might assume that more affordable financing will bring more buyers.

But lower rates could also encourage more sellers to list, making the relationship between supply and demand especially important.

Phoenix illustrates what happens when several indicators point in the same direction.

Over the latest four-week period, new pending sales declined faster than new listings. Compared with a year ago, pending activity was down nearly 17%, while new listings were slightly higher.

Meanwhile, active inventory continued to grow, and more than half of active listings had experienced a price reduction.

Together, those indicators suggest increasing competition among sellers. Fewer homes are moving into pending status, more inventory is available and price reductions are becoming more prevalent.

That doesn’t guarantee prices will fall, but it identifies conditions that could create additional pricing pressure if the trend continues.

The takeaway: Don’t evaluate pending sales, inventory or price reductions in isolation. When several indicators reinforce one another, they provide stronger evidence of where conditions may be headed. That can help sellers evaluate pricing and timing, while giving buyers a clearer picture of their negotiating position.

A seller points out that inventory is still lower than last year. Does that mean competition remains limited?

Tampa shows why the direction of recent activity matters just as much as the year-over-year comparison.

Within Tampa city limits, new listings and pending sales were essentially unchanged over the latest four weeks. Active inventory remained below last year’s level, and the share of listings with price reductions had declined.

But inventory was also beginning to increase compared with the preceding four weeks.

That doesn’t establish a market shift. Recent pending activity has been relatively steady, and price-cut share continues to ease. Still, the inventory increase is worth monitoring.

If inventory continues building while pending activity remains weak, sellers could face greater competition even before the year-over-year inventory comparison turns positive.

Try it yourself: Switch the chart above between 6M and YoY. The six-month view shows how inventory has been building recently, while the year-over-year comparison reveals that inventory remains below last year’s levels. Both are true, but each tells you something different about the market.The takeaway: A favorable market statistic doesn’t mean conditions are standing still. Track recent changes alongside year-over-year trends, and revisit pricing or buying strategies when the evidence begins to shift.

Logan’s Housing Market Tracker provides the national context. HousingWire Intelligence helps professionals connect that context to the markets where their clients are making decisions.

Use HousingWire Intelligence to chart new listings, pending sales, active inventory and price reductions in your city. Look at how the indicators move together over time, compare recent changes with the same period last year and consider what those patterns mean for the properties your clients are buying or selling.

Your clients can find national headlines and AI-generated market summaries on their own. What they need from you is the expertise to explain what those trends mean for their home, their competition and their next decision.

Methodology: HousingWire Data analysis of single-family homes across all price quartiles for the four weeks ending Oct. 30, 2026, compared with the preceding four weeks and the equivalent period in 2025. City-level figures are used for Houston, Phoenix and Tampa, with Houston metro data included for comparison. New listings and pending sales are four-week totals; inventory and price-cut shares are averages of weekly snapshots. Price-cut share represents active listings that have experienced a reduction, not reductions made during the reporting period.

Derek McInnes eager to make Rangers loan signing permanent - 'I’ve made my feelings clear on it'

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Rangers boss Derek McInnes has stressed his desire to sign James Penrice on a permanent deal.

The 27-year-old left-back joined on a season-long loan from Greek club AEK Athens in the summer and his form has earned him his first Scotland call-up.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Penrice was included in Sebastien Pocognoli’s 26-man squad for the upcoming Nations League games against North Macedonia and Switzerland.

McInnes said: “Certainly I’ve made my feelings clear on it. Ideally, I would like to have done it as a permanent, but the best way forward at the time was a loan. But I think we’re all aware of what it needs to be to make it a permanent.

“I think there’s a lot of goodwill all round to try and that could be something that we’d like to do. The conversations I’ve had with Jim (Gillespie, chief executive) and the board have been along those lines and hopefully we can make that permanent. Because I think what he’s done since the first minute is just play with so much enthusiasm, clearly wants to be here.

“Plays properly and he brings quality, but he also brings energy, the fans take to him because of how he performs and how much he wants to win. He’s one of the older ones at 27, I think. And a lot of these things are happening for him now, the first Scotland call-up and all the rest of it.

“But he’s a player that I trust and I think the supporters trust him. So hopefully we can make it a longer-term thing going forward. But that’s for the people upstairs to try and sort out.”

McInnes was pleased to see the former Partick Thistle, Livingston and Hearts defender get international recognition.

He said: “It’s great for James to get into the Scotland set-up. I think there was no real surprise with it. But nonetheless, you see James’ reaction to it and that’s the way it should be, getting called up for your country.

“Findlay (Curtis) in there as well, Liam Kelly, so we’re well represented. So it’s good for the club that they’re away with Scotland and hopefully it’s a positive experience with them.

“But I think James deserves it and hopefully he can get on the pitch and come back with a couple of caps ideally and he comes back in good form. I think the thing that screams out of James is hunger. He’s driven himself to get to where he is now.

“And I don’t think anybody should ever underestimate working with hungry players. As soon as you become too comfortable, you become complacent for me. And I think that there is a real hunger there to establish himself here. Because he knows, just from what was going on with Findlay there, that Tuur Rommens isn’t going to just move along.

“And James can play a few different positions. But he wants to be here. He plays with loads of enthusiasm and he plays with hunger and he trains with hunger. And he’s had his reward for that.”

House Democrats Eye Their Own Crypto Market Structure Bill With a Bigger SEC Role After Sweep

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

House Democrats are discussing a plan to turn Rep. Maxine Waters’ Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights into their own crypto market structure bill next Congress, with ideas that include a bigger role for the SEC and a smaller one for the CFTC, Eleanor Terrett reported Wednesday.

The proposal would fold in the Democratic Clarity Act provisions Waters offered in the House, which were not adopted, plus the provisions Senate Democrats were able to agree on with Republicans before September’s failed cloture vote. Terrett cited three industry sources who have spoken with Democratic staffers.

Further ethics provisions restricting the crypto activities of President Donald Trump and other federal officials are also likely, she wrote. Republicans are calling it a “messaging bill.”

No text for the crypto market structure bill has been released, and the details so far come from industry sources rather than from lawmakers.

JUST IN: 🇺🇸 House Democrats reportedly want to revive their Clarity Act provisions in a broader crypto market structure bill next Congress, Eleanor Terrett reports. 👀 pic.twitter.com/8twokppjHz

The talks follow Tuesday’s Democratic sweep of Congress. Waters, the top Democrat on the House Financial Services Committee, is in line to take the gavel in January from Chairman French Hill, the Clarity Act’s sponsor.

Bloomberg Law reported Wednesday that the new majorities give Democrats leverage over the Clarity Act, the industry’s top legislative priority, starting with tougher ethics rules aimed at Trump’s ventures.

Waters offered the bill of rights as amendment #31 to H.R. 3633 in July 2025. The House Rules Committee blocked it 4-8 along party lines, on a motion from Rep. Mary Gay Scanlon of Pennsylvania. Making an amendment “in order” lets the full House debate and vote on it, so the result kept the package off the floor.

The committee’s record lists what it would have required: 24/7 customer service, insurance or SIPC-like coverage, fiduciary and best-execution rules, anti-front-running rules, bankruptcy-remote custody with 24-hour withdrawals and monthly auditor-attested proof of reserves. It would also have sent more funding to the SEC and CFTC.

The committee rejected a second Democratic amendment by the same 4-8 margin. That one, from Rep. Joe Neguse of Colorado, would have barred members of Congress, presidents and vice presidents from issuing or endorsing digital assets. The ethics fight it previewed later followed the bill to the Senate.

The agency split is where a Democratic crypto market structure bill would break most sharply from the Republican one. The House-passed Clarity Act directs the CFTC to regulate most digital commodity trading, including exchanges, brokers and dealers, and exempts tokens on mature blockchains from SEC registration under certain conditions.

That design is the one the industry backed. A crypto market structure bill that moves supervision back toward the SEC would reverse it.

The Clarity Act passed the House 294-134 in July 2025 and failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15. Sen. Thom Tillis entered a motion to reconsider, which preserves the option of another vote, but the bill dies when this Congress ends on Jan. 3 unless the lame-duck session revives it.

Democrats now hold the leverage. “The industry and the Republicans screwed up. That was the best bill they were going to get,” Sen. Ruben Gallego of Arizona, a Senate Banking Committee member, said in comments reported by Bloomberg Law.

TD Cowen’s Jaret Seiberg wrote Wednesday that the sweep closes the door on Clarity for two years, and that Democratic investigations of the Trump family’s crypto ventures “may make it impossible to advance a version of Clarity that the crypto sector could back.”

A Democratic crypto market structure bill would still need Trump’s signature to become law. Whether one built around the SEC can draw enough Republican votes to get there, or remains the messaging bill Republicans say it is, is the question the next Congress opens with.

Air India CEO Wants ‘More Widebody Aircraft’

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Air India is looking to buy more widebody jets as it expands overseas and works to return to profitability.

“We want more widebody aircraft,” CEO Tewolde Gebremariam said Tuesday at the Aviation India and South Asia summit in New Delhi, according to Reuters. The carrier has held talks with both Airbus and Boeing. Gebremariam did not say how many aircraft Air India might buy.

The airline’s most recent Boeing order, announced in January, was for 30 narrowbodies: 20 737 MAX 8s and 10 MAX 10s.

The carrier is also updating the widebodies it already flies. In April, Air India took delivery of the first of 26 older Boeing 787-8s slated for cabin retrofits. The aircraft went from two cabins to three with the addition of premium economy.

Gebremariam, who previously led Ethiopian Airlines, described high operating costs as a “major concern” but said he expects Air India to return to profitability “very soon.”

Air India Group lost S$3.56 billion in the fiscal year ended March 31, according to financial disclosures from Singapore Airlines, which owns a 25% stake in the group. The loss came as the carrier dealt with disruption from the war in Iran and Pakistan’s closure of its airspace to Indian airlines.

Trump hammers Fox News, blaming network for GOP losses as ratings slide

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump turned against Fox News on Tuesday night in a string of posts on Truth Social while Democratic wins rolled in during a historic midterm election.

Trump blamed the network, which he's long championed, for Republican losses, saying in one post on his social media network that it "bears responsibility for these losses by constantly and consistently putting on the Dumocrat opposition."

Democrats took control of both the Senate and House of Representatives, flipping seats in states usually favorable to Republicans, including Texas, South Carolina and Kansas.

Trump also pointed to Fox's recent ratings as evidence of the network's decline.

"FoxNews Ratings are way down (27%!) because they're spending too much time covering the Trump Deranged Dumocrats! All of those people who used to watch Fox, and no longer do, get it. It's them, not me," he posted.

Fox News did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

In a press release last week, Fox News reported 1.1 million average viewers for October, down about 27% from the same month a year prior.

Fox Corp. CEO Lachlan Murdoch noted the decline during the company's quarterly earnings call on Monday, citing "viewer fatigue."

However, Murdoch said such a shift is expected in the lead-up to a midterm election with a second-term president and said it was not the result of viewers turning to competitors' coverage.

"Those viewers are not going to any other news channel. They're not switching to MS NOW or to CNN," he said. "They're just fatigued and watching slightly less television overall."

Versant-owned MS NOW and Skydance-owned CNN both reported primetime viewership was up by double digits in October compared with the year-ago period. However, both networks still had smaller total-day average audiences than Fox News.

MS NOW had 588,000 average viewers in October across all day parts, and CNN had 402,000, according to Nielsen data cited by Fox.

Despite Fox News' year-over-year drop in viewership, the company reported an otherwise strong quarter in what Murdoch called an "outstanding start to fiscal 2027."

Fox Corp., which includes the linear TV networks as well as entertainment and sports assets and a streaming business, reported revenue for its fiscal first quarter of $4.57 billion, up 22% year over year.

The company pointed to strength in advertising revenue, which increased 47%, buoyed by strong political advertising and the FIFA Men's World Cup, which aired in part on Fox platforms across June and July.

Trump is a frequent critic of media companies, especially broadcasters that he views as being biased against him. While he's repeatedly taken aim at NBC and Disney-owned ABC, the president has had a much friendlier relationship with Fox and its networks.

Trump appeared on Fox News' "Hannity" on Monday night ahead of the election.

Disclosure: CNBC and MS NOW are divisions of Versant Media.

Comparing Harry Kane’s incredible stats since the start of 2025-26 with Kylian Mbappe’s

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Harry Kane beat Kylian Mbappe to the Ballon d’Or in 2026 – deservedly, we reckon – and they’ll both be looking to keep up their world-class standards this season.

Kane scored more goals than anyone else across Europe’s major leagues last season, but Mbappe was the top scorer in the Champions League and the 2026 World Cup.

Kane and Mbappe faced off in last season’s Champions League quarter-finals between Bayern Munich and Real Madrid, as well as the World Cup third-place play-off between England and France.

Across their whole careers for club and country, they have a combined 998 goals.

And though neither of them have ever won the Champions League still, they are rightly regarded as two of the best players in the world.

Here’s how the two players have compared since the start of last season for their clubs.

Kane is a player we’ve become accustomed to seeing score more than 30 or 40 goals in a season, stretching back to his time with Tottenham.

But the 2025-26 campaign was the highest-scoring in his career with 61 goals, better than the 44 he got in his first season with Bayern.

He also surpassed his best-ever goalscoring campaign in the Champions League after scoring 11 in Europe last season.

Braces against Chelsea, Pafos and Union SG in the league phase helped his tally, as did a double in the second leg of their last-16 win over Atalanta.

Kane enjoyed two eight-game scoring streaks in all competitions last season and bagged hat tricks against RB Leipzig, Hoffenheim and Stuttgart in the Bundesliga.

He reached double figures of goals for the new season after 11 games.

After surpassing the 40-goal mark in both his last season with PSG and first with Real Madrid, Mbappe reached the same milestone again last season.

He will no doubt have harboured some frustrations at seeing his former teammates win the Champions League last season while Madrid made only the quarter-finals.

However, he has been on a mission since. Last season was his highest-scoring Champions League campaign; he has now overtaken Raul’s tally to enter the all-time top five competition goalscorers.

Braces against Marseille, Monaco and Benfica, a hat trick against Kairat and four goals against Olympiacos were the fruits of his efforts in last season’s league phase, before his goals in each leg against Bayern.

But Madrid’s elimination in the quarter-finals again means Mbappe will have to wait until the end of this season at least to stand a chance of finally winning the competition.

He’s started with a goal per game in it this season.

Portsmouth must take action over 22-year-old’s League One loan as Mousinho confirms clear ‘need’: Opinion

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Portsmouth signed Hayden Matthews as a highly-rated centre-back prospect in the winter of 2025. He had emerged as one to watch at Sydney FC and quickly made a good impression at Pompey too, with his performance away at Oxford United in the 2024/25 season one fondly remembered.

But, Matthews was loaned to Bradford City for the 2025/26 season after a string of injury blows and centre-back signings. Unfortunately, the 22-year-old has struggled for minutes at Valley Parade, and manager John Mousinho has stressed his need for game time.

Speaking to The News on Matthews’ situation, Mousinho said earlier in November: “He’s gone out on loan with every intention to play, but unfortunately Bradford’s (defensive) record is one of the best in the country. We’ll see what happens – they (Matthews and Josh Knight at Reading) need to play.”

Matthews is a highly promising centre-back. His time at Portsmouth has been disrupted, but there are few doubts over his potential having displayed his composure on the ball, progressive abilities in possession and knack for reading the game.

A loan to Bradford City felt ideal, allowing Matthews to play in a solid defensive setup at a level that would require him to develop his physical game. However, he didn’t get any opportunities under Graham Alexander, and his struggles have continued under Michael Duff too.

All of George Pratt, Macaulay Gillesphey, Aden Baldwin, Reece Welch and Matthew Pennington have ranked ahead of Matthews in the pecking order. Given that they have kept nine clean sheets and conceded only eight goals in 13 League One matches, it seems unlikely that the setup is changed to make way for the Pompey loan man.

Portsmouth can not allow this to continue for the entire season, and Mousinho’s words suggest they won’t. Barring a miraculous turnaround, the Championship side must take action and recall Matthews this winter.

Matthews’ Bradford City stay has not gone as hoped, and his struggle for game time may seem like a put-off for some admirers. However, the Portsmouth man is clearly a talented defender, and he has plenty of room to grow with meaningful minutes in the right setup.

It means that other League One clubs should not hesitate to move for Matthews when Pompey recall him. Ideally, he can play in a team similar to Bradford City, who prioritise a strong defensive base.

That will ensure Matthews has plenty of opportunities to develop the areas of his game he needs to focus on.

Matthews is under contract at Fratton Park until 2029, and there is still scope for him to forge a future at the Championship side. However, finding minutes to grow as a player is a must for 2027.

Flying Blue Delays 50% SAF XP Price Hike After Admitting “We Didn’t Handle The Rollout Well”

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Flying Blue has postponed an abrupt devaluation to the elite status credit members earn through sustainable aviation fuel and charitable contributions. I remain skeptical of the underlying scheme, but program chief Tiffany Funk deserves credit for acknowledging the botched rollout, listening to members, and giving them time to adjust.

Air France-KLM Flying Blue initially changed its earning rates on November 2, 2026, without advance notice. For cash contributions toward sustainable aviation fuel (SAF), the cost increased from €10 per XP to €15 per XP, a 50% increase.

XP, or Experience Points, determine Flying Blue elite status. Members who had incorporated these contributions into their qualification plans suddenly faced a more expensive path to their goal.

Now, as One Mile At A Time also reports, Flying Blue has postponed the changes until December 1, 2026. The previous earning rates return effective November 4, and contributions made since November 2 will also receive credit at the prior rates.

Tiffany Funk, Head of Flying Blue, addressed the reprieve:

On Monday, we updated how many XP you earn on Sustainable Aviation Fuel and charitable contributions. We didn’t handle the rollout well, and the new rate went live with unclear messaging and without notice. While this affects a small share of our members, we recognize for those members it matters a great deal.

The update was part of a continuous review of our elite program. We want to ensure we can keep delivering the benefits and service you expect.

For now, we’re pressing pause. From tomorrow (4 November), SAF and charitable contributions will earn XP at the previous rate, and any contribution made since 2 November will be credited at the prior rate as well. The new rate of €15 per XP begins on 1 December 2026; even at the new rate, no program in the market will recognize SAF contributions more generously than Flying Blue, including on award tickets.

We want Flying Blue to be a program you build your life around. Many of you put a lot of care into planning your flights and status qualification, and we understand your need to have time to make adjustments.

And to everyone who told us why this matters to you: thank you for caring this much about Flying Blue.

I appreciate the candor. Funk acknowledges that Flying Blue handled the rollout poorly and offers a practical correction. Members now have a transition period, including protection for contributions made during the initial rollout.

To be candid, I consider this arrangement a greenwashing revenue-generating measure. Awarding elite status credit for an environmental contribution is a convenient way to monetize the pursuit of status while wrapping the transaction in a sustainability message, a win-win for both Flying Blue and frequent travelers but probably not for the environment.

We don’t have to litigate that here and I readily acknowledge SAF can play a role in reducing aviation’s lifecycle emissions, but purchasing a contribution does not make a passenger’s flight emissions disappear. And I suspect the additional XP is the deciding factor for many members who participate, not altruism for Mother Earth.

Flying Blue certainly understands the appeal. Someone approaching a qualification deadline may find paying for a contribution far more convenient than taking another flight. But my skepticism about the arrangement does not diminish the importance of treating participating members fairly. Flying Blue offered an opportunity, and members planned around it. Their reliance was entirely foreseeable.

Flying Blue owes no one a permanent opportunity to acquire additional XP at a particular rate. It can decide that the existing formula is too generous or that it wants a different balance between flying and other qualification methods. Members can then decide whether the revised offer is worthwhile.

My larger objection, as with most devaluations, concerns candid communication and advance notice. Tell us what is changing. Give us time to adjust plans made under the existing rules. Please spare us the suggestion that a reduction in benefits is an “enhancement” inspired by member feedback.

Funk avoids that familiar gaslighting here. She does not claim members asked to pay more for their XP. She acknowledges that feedback exposed a problem with the rollout and explains what Flying Blue will do about it.

That is a much more credible use of member feedback.

Consider someone who booked travel expecting to earn a certain number of XP from flights and make up the remainder through SAF contributions. An overnight change can upset that calculation after the flights have already been purchased.

That member is not necessarily demanding that the rules remain unchanged forever. The objection is that a plan made in good faith has become more expensive without warning.

The additional time gives members an opportunity to complete contributions at the previous rate or reconsider their qualification strategy. Crediting contributions made since November 2 at the old rate also avoids penalizing those who happened to transact during the botched rollout.

Neither measure eliminates the devaluation. Both make its introduction fairer.

I commend Funk for listening and adjusting. Management can respond to criticism without abandoning the underlying business decision. Here, a relatively straightforward concession makes loyal customers  feel heard rather than taken for granted.

I remain skeptical of Flying Blue’s SAF contribution scheme, and the higher cost per XP will make it less attractive. Heck, I remain skeptical of status in general as a proud free agent for two years running. But the program has corrected the avoidable failure of introducing that change without clear communication or notice.

Funk acknowledged what went wrong and gave members time to prepare. Flying Blue is entitled to revise its earning rates. Members who planned around those rates deserve a reasonable transition, and they now have one. That is a welcome response…I never thought Funk would make the program more generous as other loyalty programs cut and devalue, but measured and clear changes with advance notice to members are all anyone can ask for.

Join our mailing list for a daily summary of posts! We never sell your info.

Matthew is an avid traveler who calls Los Angeles home. Each year he travels more than 200,000 miles by air and has visited more than 135 countries. Working both in the aviation industry and as a travel consultant, Matthew has been featured in major media outlets around the world and uses his Live and Let's Fly blog to share the latest news in the airline industry, commentary on frequent flyer programs, and detailed reports of his worldwide travel.

There’s a difference between not owing permanence in the terms of a loyalty scheme and constantly tinkering with stuff in ways that are disadvantageous to members.

Devaluing twice in the space of 1.5 year and doubling redemption rates in the process while reducing short-haul XP earnings and using opaque formulae for partner redemption (I just got quoted 48k miles for 1.5 hour in ‘business class’ with an airline which doesn’t even offer that cabin on the route in question!) are all telltale signs of a programme that just shouldn’t be trusted.
